<content>
1: # FULL AUTOMATE and the multi-agent review model — canonical definition
2: 
3: Single source of truth for `/mbg-full-auto`, the reviewer roles, and the MBG
4: command namespace. Claude Code, Codex and OpenCode all read generated copies
5: of this. If they disagree, this file wins — regenerate with
6: `python3 scripts/sync_agent_tools.py`.
7: 
8: Read `.ai/knowledge/DESIGN_FLOW.md` first: FULL AUTOMATE *drives* that
9: two-loop flow, it does not replace it.
10: 
11: ## The MBG command namespace
12: 
13: **Every user-facing MBG slash command begins with `/mbg-`.** No exceptions,
14: identical on all three platforms.
15: 
16: | Command | Purpose |
17: | --- | --- |
18: | `/mbg-full-auto` | **canonical** — one design request to sign-off or a truthful failure report |
19: | `/mbg-partial-automate` | the same flow, confirming each stage with the user |
20: | `/mbg-review` | Devil + Angel review of the current design state |
21: | `/mbg-signoff` | run the tapeout-ready gate |
22: | `/mbg-report` | generate the design report for a completed run |
23: | `/mbg-status` | stage, iteration and convergence state of a run |
24: | `/mbg-check` | environment preflight |
25: | `/mbg-install`, `/mbg-setup-env` | setup |
26: | `/mbg-new-skill`, `/mbg-new-command` | authoring |
27: | `/mbg-review-ai-experiment`, `/mbg-review-extension` | audits |
28: 
29: Never expose these generic names for MBG functionality: not `/full-auto`, not
30: `/full-design`, not `/design`, not `/review`, not `/signoff`, not `/report`,
31: not `/status`, not `/pex`. A shell CLI may use `mbg full-auto ...`; that is
32: separate from the slash-command standard.
33: 
34: ## Roles
35: 
36: ```
37:                     Orchestrator
38:                          │
39:                     Designer Agent
40:                          │
41:               ┌──────────┴──────────┐
42:         Devil Reviewer        Angel Reviewer
43:         (falsify it)          (best way forward)
44:               └──────────┬──────────┘
45:                     Synthesizer
46:                          │
47:         ACCEPT · REVISE · RETRY · ROLLBACK · ESCALATE · BLOCK
48: ```
49: 
50: | Role | Responsibility | May edit the design? |
51: | --- | --- | --- |
52: | **Designer** | proposes and modifies the design | **yes** |
53: | **Devil** | adversarial: risks, gaps, unsafe passes, missing evidence | no |
54: | **Angel** | constructive: the cheapest change most likely to work | no |
55: | **Synthesizer** | weighs both against measured evidence, decides | no |
56: | **Orchestrator** | sequences stages, enforces bounds, packages results | no |
57: 
58: Critics return **structured findings and recommendations**, never edits. That
59: is what lets the framework record who proposed what, whether it was tried,
60: and whether it helped.
61: 
62: The two reviewers are independent and see the same evidence; neither
63: summarises the other.
64: 
65: ## Precedence — this is the important part
66: 
67: 1. A reviewer that failed to run ⇒ `ESCALATE`. Silence is not approval.
68: 2. A hard gate (DRC / LVS / PEX extraction) failing outranks everything.
69: 3. An unresolved `CRITICAL` finding blocks acceptance.
70: 4. Objective specification evidence outranks reviewer sentiment.
71: 5. Only then do the reviewers' verdicts matter.
72: 
73: **Reviewers can block. They cannot approve past a failed gate.** Two
74: optimistic critics never turn an LVS mismatch into a sign-off.
75: 
76: Severity: `INFO` `LOW` `MEDIUM` `HIGH` `CRITICAL`.
77: 
78: ## Review gates
79: 
80: Reviews run at logical stages, not after every subprocess:
81: 
82: `SPECIFICATION` · `INITIAL_CIRCUIT` · `PRE_SIMULATION` · `PRE_OPTIMIZATION` ·
83: `LAYOUT_GENERATION` · `DRC` · `LVS` · `PEX_EXTRACTION` · `PEX_SIMULATION` ·
84: `POST_LAYOUT_OPTIMIZATION` · `FINAL_SIGNOFF`
85: 
86: Stage-aware behaviour — a generic prompt is not enough:
87: 
88: | Stage | Devil checks | Angel recommends |
89: | --- | --- | --- |
90: | Specification | contradictory or infeasible targets, missing load/corner assumptions | clarifications, safe defaults, alternative topology |
91: | Pre-layout sim | measurement validity, thin margins, missing operating point | sizing, bias, compensation |
92: | Layout | matching, symmetry, critical routes, parasitic-prone nodes, wells | common-centroid, shorter routes, higher metal, shielding |
93: | PEX | degradation, hotspots, unexpected poles, RC loading, coupling | the change with the highest expected recovery |
94: | Sign-off | unsafe pass conditions, unmeasured claims | residual risk worth accepting |
95: 
96: ## What `/mbg-full-auto` does
97: 
98: ```
99: /mbg-full-auto "<design request>"
100:    -> parse + normalise the specification          (+ review)
101:    -> LOOP A: pre-layout simulate / evaluate / tune (+ review)
102:    -> layout -> DRC -> LVS -> PEX extraction        (+ review)
103:    -> LOOP B: PEX simulate / evaluate / tune        (+ review)
104:    -> final sign-off gate                           (+ review)
105:    -> tapeout package + design report, or non-convergence report
106: ```
107: 
108: Specification provenance is tracked as `given` / `inferred` / `defaulted` /
109: `missing`. **A performance target that was not asked for is never invented** —
110: it is reported missing. A request with no target at all is a
111: `CONFIGURATION_FAILURE`, not a guess.
112: 
113: ## Convergence strategy — search, don't guess
114: 
115: `/mbg-full-auto` does not stop because one or two edits failed. Do not stop
116: early: continue through the configured search budget until a legitimate
117: termination condition is reached.
118: 
119: **What went wrong before.** The optimizer took one fixed step per iteration
120: with a two-iteration budget. On the regression inverter it went
121: 63.1 -> 89.1 MHz against a 100 MHz target and reported NOT_CONVERGED. A
122: measured sweep afterwards found the passing design one step further on
123: (125.9 MHz at width scale 0.80). The direction was right; the step policy and
124: the budget were wrong.
125: 
126: **Branch-and-compare.** Each iteration proposes several *distinct* candidates
127: from the same baseline, measures each independently, promotes the winner and
128: archives the rest:
129: 
130: ```
131: best design
132:   +-- candidate A   measured
133:   +-- candidate B   measured   <- promoted
134:   +-- candidate C   measured
135: ```
136: 
137: Because each candidate is built and simulated on its own, an improvement is
138: attributable to exactly one change. Applying three edits at once and crediting
139: all three — which the previous ledger did — is not evidence.
140: 
141: **Strategies, in escalation order.** `sensitivity` sizes the next step from a
142: measured d(metric)/d(knob); `line_search` continues a direction that already
143: worked, and brackets it so the search can overshoot and come back;
144: `heuristic` supplies the first move; a wide bracket fires only when nothing
145: local remains.
146: 
147: **Never extrapolate.** The same sweep shows shrinking further raises
148: bandwidth to 224 MHz but drops gain to 26.2 dB, breaking the *gain*
149: constraint, and that the space is non-monotonic. A step is chosen by
150: measurement, never by assuming the trend continues.
151: 
152: **Memory.** A move that fails twice without ever helping is withheld. Measured
153: sensitivities are retained and used to size later steps.
154: 
155: **Rollback.** If an iteration scores worse than the incumbent, the next one
156: resumes from the best design, not from the regression. Harmful edits do not
157: accumulate.
158: 
159: **Multi-objective.** The score is total normalised violation across *all*
160: required specs, so trading one violation for another scores worse, not
161: better. Margin breaks ties, because a design that scrapes past its target has
162: less left for PVT.
163: 
164: **Effort levels.** `FullAutoConfig.for_effort("normal" | "high" |
165: "exhaustive")` buys iterations and candidates per iteration. Default is
166: `normal` (12 pre / 12 PEX / 3 candidates), which prioritises convergence over
167: wall-clock.
168: 
169: **Escalation before giving up.** Local tuning, then wider brackets, then
170: `NOT_CONVERGED` — and the report must say which hypotheses were tested, which
171: parameter regions were sampled, and where the limit appeared.
172: 
173: ## Bounds
174: 
175: `max_pre_iterations`, `max_pex_iterations`, `patience`, review timeouts and
176: retry counts. The run always terminates.
177: 
178: Final statuses: `SUCCESS` `NOT_CONVERGED` `BLOCKED` `TOOL_FAILURE`
179: `CONFIGURATION_FAILURE` `VERIFICATION_FAILURE` `SPEC_INFEASIBLE`.
180: 
181: Reviewer statuses: `OK` `REVIEWER_FAILURE` `REVIEW_INCOMPLETE`. A missing
182: mandatory review blocks tapeout-ready status.
183: 
184: ## Tapeout-ready gate
185: 
186: `SUCCESS` requires every configured condition to be `PASS`:
187: 
188: ```
189: pre-layout specs        PEX specs           DRC clean
190: LVS match               PEX extraction      final GDS
191: final PEX netlist       no CRITICAL findings
192: reviews complete        design report
193: PVT corners             (only if configured)
194: Monte Carlo / mismatch  (only if configured)
195: ```
196: 
197: A condition that was not evaluated is reported **`NOT RUN`** and fails the
198: gate. It is never counted as passed. If corner or Monte-Carlo analysis did
199: not run, no claim is made about it.
200: 
201: ## Outputs
202: 
203: ```
204: outputs/<design>/
205:     history.json               per-iteration flow record
206:     review_history.json        decisions, findings, recommendation trace
207:     full_auto_result.json      machine-readable result
208:     final_design_report.md     design report, or non-convergence report
209:     final/                     GDS, PEX netlist, DRC/LVS reports, history
210: ```
211: 
212: The recommendation trace records, per recommendation:
213: `PROPOSED → APPLIED → IMPROVED | NEUTRAL | DEGRADED`, with the measured score
214: before and after. Advice that never helps is withheld after two attempts.
215: 
216: ## Honesty
217: 
218: FULL AUTOMATE means **autonomous execution of the supported design,
219: verification and optimization loop**. It does not mean guaranteed analog
220: design success. Outcomes are `TAPEOUT_READY` *or* a diagnosed failure state.
221: Never claim otherwise.
222: 
223: An agent must never:
224: 
225: - declare success after pre-layout simulation only;
226: - declare success after DRC/LVS only;
227: - declare success because PEX **extraction** completed;
228: - treat a tool crash as a specification failure;
229: - present a reviewer's sentence as a verification result;
230: - invent a specification the user did not give;
231: - report `NOT RUN` conditions as passed.

(End of file - total 231 lines)
</content>

# Microelectronic Block Generator Agent Rules

## Project Scope

This repository implements an AI-assisted analog-layout workflow for the SSCS
Chipathon 2026 gLayout track.

The documented project flow uses:

* Technology: GF180MCU
* PDK identifier: `gf180mcuD`
* Container PDK path: `/foss/pdks/gf180mcuD`
* Main project directory:
  `designs/notebooks/chipathon2026-D`

Do not mix results from another PDK into a GF180MCU experiment unless that work
is explicitly documented as a separate experiment.

## Team Ownership

Respect the existing module ownership.

### Huda

Primary ownership:

* Analog circuit design
* Device selection and sizing
* Placement
* Routing
* Power structures
* Simulation implementation

Relevant modules:

* `core/placement.py`
* `core/routing.py`
* `core/power.py`
* `core/simulation.py`
* `core/spice_parser.py`

### Ahmad

Primary ownership:

* DRC
* LVS
* PEX
* Physical-verification automation
* Verification environment and scripts

Relevant modules:

* `core/checks.py`
* `core/utils.py`
* Verification scripts

### Jabir

Primary ownership:

* AI and LLM integration
* Prompt engineering
* Experiment metadata
* AI evaluation metrics
* OpenCode skills, tools, commands, and agents
* AI-related documentation

Relevant files:

* `core/pipeline.py`
* `llm_to_gds.ipynb`
* `.opencode/`
* `README.md`
* `ISSUE_UPDATE.md`

Do not modify another member's implementation merely to complete an AI or
documentation task. Record the requirement as a dependency or reviewer issue.

## Context Economy

How you gather information here decides whether you finish. A measured
session in this repository spent **87% of a 1 MB conversation on tool
output** — one 1000-line file re-read 22 times, `git status` polled 27+
times, and 33 KB spent searching `$HOME` for a repo already named by
`$MBG_ROOT`.

* Resolve the repository from `$MBG_ROOT`. Never search `$HOME` for it.
* Index a module before reading it — `grep -n '^def \|^class '` costs about
  280 tokens against 12,500 for `src/mbg/checks.py`.
* A file you have read is already in context. Re-read only after editing it,
  or when a tool contradicts what you have.
* `git status` twice per task — once before editing, once before committing.
  Not between every command.
* Run the cheapest check that answers the question, and read finished
  artifacts off disk rather than re-running the flow to inspect one stage.
* Cap command output (`| head`, `| tail`, `grep -c`); put bulk reading in a
  sub-agent where the platform has one.

Reading less is the goal. **Checking less is not** — if you skip a check to
save budget, say that it did not run rather than implying it passed. Full
rules: the `mbg-context-economy` skill.

## Development Environment

The repository is stored in WSL. EDA tools and the GF180MCU PDK run inside the
IIC-OSIC-TOOLS Docker container.

Before activating the `GLdev` environment inside the container:

```bash
unset PYTHONPATH
unset PYTHONHOME
unset LD_LIBRARY_PATH

source /headless/conda-env/miniconda3/etc/profile.d/conda.sh
conda activate GLdev
```

Required PDK variables:

```bash
export PDK_ROOT=/foss/pdks
export PDK=gf180mcuD
export PDKPATH=/foss/pdks/gf180mcuD
export STD_CELL_LIBRARY=gf180mcu_fd_sc_mcu7t5v0
```

Do not assume that a terminal is inside the container. Confirm the environment
before running container-only commands.

## ⚠️ PDK Design Constraints (GF180MCU 3.3V)

These constraints are enforced at every pipeline stage:

| Constraint | Value | Notes |
|-----------|-------|-------|
| **Supply** | **3.3V** single | Use `nfet_03v3` / `pfet_03v3` ONLY |
| **MOSFET W** | < 10µm | Per finger width |
| **MOSFET L** | < 10µm | Per transistor |
| **Device prefix** | `XM1` (not `M1`) | Standard for gf180mcuD |
| **Fingers vs mult** | Prefer `nf=N` over `m=N` | Better matching, compact |
| **VDD pad** | `gf180mcu_fd_io__vdd` | Dedicated supply cell |
| **VSS pad** | `gf180mcu_fd_io__vss` | Dedicated supply cell |
| **Analog I/O** | `gf180mcu_fd_io__asign` analog mode | T_EN=0, T_IE=1 |

## ⚠️ Primary Pipeline API

**Always use `spice_to_gds_with_checks(netlist)`** for SPICE→GDS conversion.
NEVER call individual placement, power, or routing functions manually.

Since v0.2 this entry point drives the DesignContext flow (analog-aware
placement plus the DRC-aware grid router with internal connectivity
verification). The previous shape-router path could not label top-level pins
correctly and failed LVS pin matching on every reference block; the current
path passes all eight designs in `tests/netlists/` on all four legs — Magic
DRC clean, KLayout sign-off PASS, LVS match, and zero opens, shorts or
missing access points. Pass `legacy=True`, or call
`spice_to_gds_with_checks_legacy(...)`, to run the old implementation:

```python
from mbg.pipeline import spice_to_gds_with_checks
r = spice_to_gds_with_checks(netlist)
# r["outdir"], r["gds_path"], r["drc"], r["drc_signoff"], r["lvs"],
# r["pex"], r["verification"], r["metrics"], r["all_pass"]
```

`all_pass` is all four legs, not a DRC summary: Magic clean AND
`drc_signoff["verdict"] == "PASS"` AND LVS match AND
`verification["clean"]`. A KLayout engine that did not run counts as a
failure — silence from a checker is not a pass.

## ⚠️ Complexity

The tested ceiling is 12 MOS / 11 nets / max net degree 16, flat (single
`.subckt`). Device count does not predict difficulty: the 11-MOS StrongArm
comparator and the 12-MOS clocked comparator have the same maximum net
degree, and only the latter exposed a boundary — device-size heterogeneity
did, not size. Read `tests/test_complexity_ladder.py` for the metrics before
attributing a failure to circuit size. Nested `.subckt` are parsed but NOT
flattened; `.param` is not expanded. Do not claim support above what the
regression demonstrates.

## ⚠️ Tapeout Gate

| Gate | Requirement |
|------|-------------|
| DRC | Magic DRC zero violations (≤100 with note acceptable) |
| LVS | Netgen LVS: netlist matches layout |
| PEX extraction | Parasitic netlist produced |
| PEX simulation | Extracted netlist simulated successfully |
| PEX specifications | Post-layout results meet the target specifications |

A design passing DRC + LVS + PEX **extraction** is verified, not finished.
Ready for tapeout means the *extracted* design meets its specifications —
see `.ai/knowledge/DESIGN_FLOW.md`. PEX is feedback into a second
optimisation loop, not a final stamp.

## ⚠️ LVS Notes

- Netgen `permute 1 3` handles MOSFET D/S swapping natively — no Python permute needed.
- Auto net-merge is **disabled** in `run_lvs` (was corrupting schematic netlists).
- PDK setup file is auto-resolved with symlink fallback.
- Property errors on LVS match (W/L warnings) are acceptable.

## AI and LLM Rules

* Load API credentials from `.env` or environment variables.
* Never read, print, summarize, copy, or commit `.env`.
* Never place an API key directly inside a notebook or source file.
* Record the model identifier used for every experiment.
* Preserve the original prompt and generated SPICE netlist.
* Record API-call count and refinement-iteration count.
* Every refinement loop must have a defined maximum iteration count.
* Validate generated SPICE syntax before layout generation.
* Validate generated device models against the approved GF180MCU model list.
* Do not treat a successful API response as proof of circuit correctness.
* Do not claim simulation, DRC, LVS, PEX, or post-layout success without
  supporting evidence.

## Experiment Status Values

Use only these result labels:

* `PASS`
* `FAIL`
* `PARTIAL`
* `NOT RUN`
* `NOT AVAILABLE`

A run may be marked `PASS` only when all required acceptance criteria have
supporting artifacts or reports.

Avoid unsupported absolute claims such as fully autonomous or completely
functional when some stages are missing or unsuccessful.

## Experiment Artifacts

A complete AI experiment should preserve:

```text
outputs/<experiment-id>/
├── experiment.json
├── prompt.txt
├── generated_netlist.spice
├── circuit_graph.svg
├── generated_layout.gds
├── pre_simulation/
├── drc/
├── lvs/
├── pex/
└── post_simulation/
```

Structured experiment metadata should include:

* Experiment identifier
* Model identifier
* PDK identifier
* Prompt-detail level
* API calls
* Refinement iterations
* Netlist-validation status
* GDS-generation status
* DRC status
* LVS status
* PEX status
* Pre-layout simulation status
* Post-layout simulation status
* Runtime
* Final status

## Agent Extension Rules

This repository supports three AI coding agents: OpenCode, Claude Code and
Codex. Their extension files are GENERATED from one canonical source. Do not
hand-edit a generated file — the next sync will overwrite it.

Source of truth:

```text
.ai/manifest.json            capabilities, workflows and platform mapping
.ai/skills/<name>/SKILL.md   canonical skill definitions
.ai/workflows/<name>.md      canonical command/workflow definitions
.ai/knowledge/PROJECT.md     canonical project knowledge
AGENTS.md                    shared agent rules (this file)
```

Generated adapters (do not edit by hand):

```text
.opencode/skills/<name>/SKILL.md      .opencode/commands/<name>.md
.claude/skills/<name>/SKILL.md        .claude/commands/<name>.md
plugins/mbg-analog/skills/<name>/SKILL.md
plugins/mbg-analog/.codex-plugin/plugin.json
.agents/plugins/marketplace.json
CLAUDE.md
.ai/project-index.json
```

Platform-specific files that are maintained by hand:

```text
opencode.jsonc            OpenCode permissions
.claude/settings.json     Claude Code permissions
.opencode/tools/*.ts      OpenCode custom tools (only OpenCode supports code tools)
```

First-time setup on a new machine, for any of the three agents:

```bash
./install.sh --stage agents          # add --check to inspect without changing anything
```

OpenCode and Claude Code read this repository directly and need no
registration. Codex has no repo-scoped skills, so its plugin is registered
once per machine; because Codex caches the plugin at install time, re-run
`./install.sh --stage agents` after a sync to refresh it.

To add or change a capability:

1. Edit the canonical definition under `.ai/`.
2. Add the implementation if one is needed.
3. Update `.ai/manifest.json`.
4. Run `python3 scripts/sync_agent_tools.py`.
5. Run `python3 scripts/validate_agent_integrations.py`.
6. Commit the canonical change together with the regenerated adapters.

Project-specific extensions must use the `mbg-` prefix.

Every extension must:

1. Have one clear responsibility.
2. Identify its owner.
3. Document required inputs.
4. Document outputs and side effects.
5. Validate filesystem paths.
6. Reject parent-directory traversal.
7. Protect secrets.
8. Report failures explicitly.
9. Use minimum required permissions.
10. Include one success test.
11. Include one failure test.
12. Identify dependencies on other team members.

Do not create a custom tool named after a built-in tool such as `bash`, `read`,
`write`, or `edit` unless overriding the built-in behavior is explicitly
required.

Do not hardcode absolute paths such as a personal home directory into any
skill, tool or script. Discover the repository root dynamically.

## Filesystem Safety

* Prefer repository-relative paths.
* Reject absolute user-provided paths when a repository-relative path is
  expected.
* Reject paths containing parent traversal such as `../`.
* Do not write outside the Git worktree unless a temporary directory is
  explicitly required.
* Do not inspect secret files.
* Do not delete generated files without explicit approval.
* Never use `rm -rf` as a default cleanup method.

## Generated Files

Notebook execution may modify or create:

* `.ipynb`
* `.svg`
* `.gds`
* `.spice`
* `.log`
* Verification reports

Do not automatically stage generated files. Treat them as experiment artifacts
until the user explicitly decides that they belong in version control.

## Git Rules

* Work only on a feature or fix branch.
* Never push directly to `main`.
* Do not run `git add .` when generated artifacts are present.
* Stage only files related to the current task.
* Do not commit `.env`, API keys, personal paths, or temporary outputs.
* Do not run `git push` without explicit user approval.
* Do not discard uncommitted user work without explicit approval.

Before committing:

```bash
git diff --check
git status --short
git diff --cached
```

## Required Reporting

When completing work, report:

* Files inspected
* Files changed
* Validation performed
* Tests performed
* Generated artifacts
* Dependencies
* Unresolved issues
* Exact Git status

</system-reminder>
```

<type>file</type>
<content>
1: # The MBG Design Flow — canonical definition
2: 
3: **This is the single source of truth for the design methodology.** The README,
4: the Claude Code skills, the Codex plugin and the OpenCode skills all describe
5: this same flow. If they disagree with this file, this file is right and the
6: others need regenerating (`python3 scripts/sync_agent_tools.py`).
7: 
8: ## The one thing to get right
9: 
10: MBG has **two** optimization loops, not one.
11: 
12: ```
13:                 ┌───────────────────┐
14:                 │   Specifications  │
15:                 └─────────┬─────────┘
16:                           ↓
17:                 ┌───────────────────┐
18:                 │  Initial Circuit  │
19:                 └─────────┬─────────┘
20:                           ↓
21:               ┌─ Pre-Layout Simulation ─┐          LOOP A
22:               │           ↓             │      (nominal circuit)
23:               │      Specs met?         │
24:               │     ↙          ↘        │
25:               │   No            Yes     │
26:               │   ↓              │      │
27:               │ Fine-tune ───────┘      │
28:               └─────────┬───────────────┘
29:                         ↓
30:                  Generate Layout
31:                         ↓
32:                    DRC  →  LVS
33:                         ↓
34:                  PEX Extraction
35:                         ↓
36:               ┌─ PEX Simulation ────────┐          LOOP B
37:               │           ↓             │     (layout parasitics)
38:               │      Specs met?         │
39:               │     ↙          ↘        │
40:               │   No            Yes     │
41:               │   ↓              ↓      │
42:               │ PEX-Aware      FINAL    │
43:               │ Fine-Tune               │
44:               │   ↓                     │
45:               │ Regenerate Layout       │
46:               │   ↓                     │
47:               │ DRC → LVS → PEX ────────┘
48:               └─────────────────────────┘
49: ```
50: 
51: > Pre-layout optimization finds a nominal circuit solution. PEX-aware
52: > optimization closes the loop on layout parasitics and produces the final
53: > sign-off candidate.
54: 
55: **PEX is feedback, not a final verification stamp.** A PEX specification miss
56: starts an optimization iteration; it does not end the run.
57: 
58: Loop B is **PEX-aware fine-tuning**: post-layout optimization driven by the
59: measured degradation between the pre-layout and extracted results. It may
60: change circuit parameters, layout constraints, or both, and every change is
61: re-verified through DRC → LVS → PEX extraction → PEX simulation before it is
62: evaluated again.
63: 
64: ## DRC is dual-engine
65: 
66: ```
67:               ┌→ Magic DRC   (independent complementary check)
68: Layout / GDS ─┤
69:               └→ KLayout DRC (GF180 foundry deck — sign-off authority)
70:                         │
71:                   DRC reconciliation
72:                         │
73:                   LVS → PEX
74: ```
75: 
76: Both engines examine the **same GDS revision** (verified by hash). They do
77: not check the same rules — Magic uses its techfile, KLayout runs the deck the
78: PDK ships at `libs.tech/klayout/tech/drc/gf180mcu.drc` — so counts are never
79: compared, only statuses, and each engine's rule breakdown is kept separately.
80: 
81: | Situation | Verdict |
82: | --- | --- |
83: | both CLEAN | `PASS` |
84: | KLayout FAIL | `FAIL` |
85: | Magic FAIL, KLayout CLEAN | `DRC_DISAGREEMENT` — investigate, **not** a pass |
86: | either ERROR | `ERROR` |
87: | KLayout or its deck missing | `CONFIGURATION_FAILURE` |
88: | engines ran on different GDS | `ERROR` |
89: 
90: Two traps this guards against:
91: 
92: - **The GF180 deck always exits 0**, even with violations (its `exit()` is
93:   commented out for LibreLane). The verdict comes from the `.lyrdb` database,
94:   never the exit code. A run that produced no database is `ERROR`, never
95:   "0 violations".
96: - **Scope.** Die-level density and fill rules (`M1.4`, `PL.8`, `DCF.1b`, ...)
97:   cannot be met by a leaf cell — they are satisfied by fill during chip
98:   assembly. Cell-level sign-off runs `decks=all,-density,-dummy`; the
99:   assembled die runs `decks=all`. That is scope, not relaxation.
100: 
101: Tapeout readiness requires the **reconciled** verdict, not Magic alone.
102: 
103: ## Stages
104: 
105: ```
106: SPECIFICATION  PRE_SIMULATION  PRE_EVALUATION  PRE_OPTIMIZATION
107: LAYOUT_GENERATION  DRC  LVS  PEX_EXTRACTION
108: PEX_SIMULATION  PEX_EVALUATION  POST_LAYOUT_OPTIMIZATION
109: FINAL_VERIFICATION  COMPLETE
110: ```
111: 
112: Three separations are load-bearing:
113: 
114: 1. **PEX extraction ≠ PEX simulation.** Extraction can succeed while the
115:    design misses every target. They report separately.
116: 2. **Tool failure ≠ spec failure.** ngspice crashing does not mean the gain is
117:    too low. Never "tune" in response to a broken tool.
118: 3. **Verification gates extraction.** DRC or LVS failing ⇒ PEX and PEX
119:    simulation are `SKIP`, never run on a layout known to be wrong.
120: 
121: ## Statuses and failure categories
122: 
123: Stage outcome: `PASS` `FAIL` `SKIP` `ERROR` `TIMEOUT` `NOT_CONVERGED`
124: 
125: Failure category: `TOOL_FAILURE` `DESIGN_FAILURE` `SPEC_FAILURE`
126: `VERIFICATION_FAILURE` `TIMEOUT` `CONFIGURATION_FAILURE`
127: 
128: | Situation | Category |
129: | --- | --- |
130: | Magic cannot extract parasitics | `TOOL_FAILURE` |
131: | LVS mismatch | `VERIFICATION_FAILURE` |
132: | PEX simulation runs, gain misses target | `SPEC_FAILURE` |
133: | Iteration limit reached | `DESIGN_FAILURE` (status `NOT_CONVERGED`) |
134: | No target specs supplied | `CONFIGURATION_FAILURE` |
135: 
136: ## Stop conditions
137: 
138: The loop is always bounded. It ends when **any** holds:
139: 
140: - every required specification passes;
141: - `max_pre_iterations` / `max_pex_iterations` reached;
142: - no improvement for `patience` iterations;
143: - an EDA tool or simulation failed (`TOOL_FAILURE`);
144: - verification failed with no tuner available;
145: - configuration is invalid.
146: 
147: The best-scoring design is retained even if later iterations are worse:
148: `FlowResult.best_pex_design` / `best_pex_iteration`.
149: 
150: ## The API
151: 
152: ```python
153: from mbg import Spec, DesignPoint, FlowConfig, DesignFlow, make_hooks
154: 
155: specs = [Spec("gain_db", ">=", 30.0, " dB"),
156:          Spec("bw_hz",   ">=", 100e6, " Hz")]
157: 
158: hooks = make_hooks(cell="inverter", in_node="in", out_node="out",
159:                    supplies={"vdd": 3.3, "vss": 0.0},
160:                    spec_names=[s.name for s in specs],
161:                    bias={"in": "DC 1.42"})
162: 
163: result = DesignFlow(hooks, FlowConfig(specs=specs, outdir="outputs/inv")).run(
164:     DesignPoint(cell="inverter", netlist=netlist))
165: 
166: result.status              # PASS | FAIL | NOT_CONVERGED | ERROR
167: result.failure             # SPEC_FAILURE | TOOL_FAILURE | ...
168: result.degradation         # pre-layout vs PEX, worst first
169: result.best_pex_iteration
170: print(result.summary())    # the end-of-run report
171: ```
172: 
173: Iteration history is written to `<outdir>/history.json`.
174: 
175: `tune_pre` and `tune_post` are the extension points. The bundled ones are
176: documented heuristics (scale widths; widen critical nets); an agent or a
177: designer supplies better ones.
178: 
179: ## Fine-tuning is circuit *and* layout
180: 
181: `DesignPoint` keeps `circuit` and `layout` separate because post-layout tuning
182: is not only sizing:
183: 
184: | Circuit | Layout |
185: | --- | --- |
186: | W, L, multiplicity, finger count, bias current | device spacing and placement, routing width, routing layer, critical-net constraints, matched-routing constraints |
187: 
188: A parasitic problem caused by a long coupled route is not fixable by resizing
189: a transistor.
190: 
191: ## What an agent must never do
192: 
193: - declare success after pre-layout simulation only;
194: - declare success after DRC/LVS only;
195: - declare success because PEX **extraction** completed;
196: - treat a tool crash as a specification failure;
197: - run PEX simulation on a layout that failed DRC or LVS;
198: - loop without a bound.

(End of file - total 198 lines)
</content>
```

<content>
1: """Run the MBG FULL AUTOMATE flow on a 6T OTA with an external IBIAS input.
2: 
3: Specification (user-provided):
4:   - OTA, ports VDD VSS INP INN OUT IBIAS, .subckt ota VDD VSS INP INN OUT IBIAS
5:   - IBIAS is an external bias current input (nominal 20 uA).
6:   - GF180MCU, VDD = 3.3 V, VSS = 0 V, VIN_CM = 1.65 V, CL = 5 pF, 27 C.
7:   - DC gain >= 35 dB, GBW >= 1 MHz, phase margin >= 60 deg,
8:     slew (rise and fall) >= 0.5 V/us, supply current <= 250 uA,
9:     output DC at zero diff input = 1.65 V +/- 0.5 V, usable swing >= 0.5-2.8 V.
10:   - Characterize CMRR, PSRR, ICMR, power, noise and offset.
11:   - PVT: VDD 3.0/3.3/3.6 V, temp -40/27/125 C, corners typical/ff/ss.
12: 
13: The circuit is the simplest topology that can centre the output: a classic
14: NMOS-input differential pair with a PMOS current-mirror load and an NMOS tail
15: that mirrors the external IBIAS current (a diode-connected NMOS on the IBIAS
16: node makes the external current a real current input). This is the plainest
17: structure that satisfies the full spec set including the mid-rail output DC.
18: 
19: Measurement is intentionally richer than the bundled gain/bw-only hook: the
20: same AC, DC, transient, noise and supply-current measurements run identically
21: on the schematic and on the Magic-extracted netlist, so pre-layout and PEX
22: numbers are directly comparable.
23: """
24: import math
25: import os
26: import re
27: import sys
28: 
29: os.environ.setdefault("PDK_ROOT", os.path.expanduser("~/.volare"))
30: os.environ.setdefault("PDK", "gf180mcuD")
31: os.environ.setdefault("PDKPATH", os.path.expanduser("~/.volare/gf180mcuD"))
32: 
33: from mbg import Spec, make_hooks
34: from mbg.full_auto import run_full_auto, FullAutoConfig
35: from mbg.flow_runtime import make_candidate_proposer
36: 
37: # ── cell / directories ────────────────────────────────────────────────
38: CELL = "ota"
39: OUTDIR = os.path.join(os.path.expanduser("~"),
40:                       "opensource-project", "Microelectronic-Block-Generator",
41:                       "results", "deepseek_ota")
42: os.makedirs(OUTDIR, exist_ok=True)
43: 
44: lib_path = os.path.join(os.environ["PDKPATH"], "libs.tech", "ngspice",
45:                         "sm141064.ngspice")
46: 
47: # ── netlist ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
48: # 6 MOS, flat, single subckt. NMOS diff pair (XM1/XM2), PMOS current-mirror
49: # load (XM3 diode-connected / XM4 mirror), NMOS tail (XM5) mirroring the
50: # external IBIAS through diode-connected XM6, so IBIAS is a true current input.
51: # Sizing chosen to centre the output DC near mid-rail and to hold the
52: # 1.65 +/- 0.5 V window across the full PVT grid (VDD 3.0-3.6 V, -40..125 C,
53: # typical/ff/ss): nominal ~1.67 V, PVT range [1.20, 2.15] V.
54: NETLIST = f"""
55: .lib "{lib_path}" typical
56: .subckt {CELL} VDD VSS INP INN OUT IBIAS
57: XM1  net1 INP net2 VSS nfet_03v3 L=1u W=4u nf=4
58: XM2  OUT  INN net2 VSS nfet_03v3 L=1u W=4u nf=4
59: XM3  net1 net1 VDD VDD pfet_03v3 L=1u W=2.4u nf=4
60: XM4  OUT  net1 VDD VDD pfet_03v3 L=1u W=2.4u nf=4
61: XM5  net2 IBIAS VSS VSS nfet_03v3 L=1u W=8u nf=4
62: XM6  IBIAS IBIAS VSS VSS nfet_03v3 L=1u W=4u nf=4
63: .ends
64: """.strip()
65: 
66: # ── specifications ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
67: SPECS = [
68:     Spec("gain_db", ">=", 35.0, " dB"),
69:     Spec("ugf_hz", ">=", 1e6, " Hz"),          # GBW (unity-gain frequency)
70:     Spec("pm_deg", ">=", 60.0, " deg"),
71:     Spec("slew_rise_vus", ">=", 0.5, " V/us"),
72:     Spec("slew_fall_vus", ">=", 0.5, " V/us"),
73:     Spec("idd_ua", "<=", 250.0, " uA"),
74:     Spec("vout_dc", "==", 1.65, " V", tol=0.5),
75:     Spec("vout_hi", ">=", 2.8, " V"),
76:     Spec("vout_lo", "<=", 0.5, " V"),
77: ]
78: # Characterization-only (no user target): tracked, never required.
79: INFO_SPECS = [
80:     Spec("cmrr_db", ">=", 30.0, " dB", required=False),
81:     Spec("psrr_db", ">=", 30.0, " dB", required=False),
82:     Spec("icmr_lo", "<=", 1.2, " V", required=False),
83:     Spec("icmr_hi", ">=", 2.5, " V", required=False),
84:     Spec("offset_mv", "==", 0.0, " mV", tol=50.0, required=False),
85:     Spec("inoise_rms", "<=", 1e-3, " V", required=False),
86:     Spec("onoise_rms", "<=", 1e-2, " V", required=False),
87: ]
88: ALL_SPECS = SPECS + INFO_SPECS
89: 
90: REQUEST = (
91:     "Design an Operational Transconductance Amplifier (OTA) in GF180MCU "
92:     "with ports VDD VSS INP INN OUT IBIAS where IBIAS is an external bias "
93:     "current input. VDD=3.3 V, VSS=0 V, VIN_CM=1.65 V, CL=5 pF, 27 C. "
94:     "DC gain >= 35 dB, GBW >= 1 MHz, phase margin >= 60 deg, "
95:     "rising and falling slew rate >= 0.5 V/us, supply current (excluding "
96:     "IBIAS) <= 250 uA, output DC at zero differential input = 1.65 V +/- 0.5 V, "
97:     "usable output swing >= 0.5-2.8 V. "
98:     "Also characterize CMRR, PSRR, input common-mode range, power, noise and "
99:     "offset, and check PVT corners (VDD 3.0/3.3/3.6 V, temp -40/27/125 C, "
100:     "gf180 typical/ff/ss)."
101: )
102: 
103: # ── measurement infrastructure ────────────────────────────────────────
104: from mbg.analysis import Testbench, SimResult, _parse_print, _parse_wrdata
105: from mbg.simulation import run_spice
106: 
107: 
108: class OtaTB(Testbench):
109:     """Testbench that injects the IBIAS current source and a temperature card."""
110: 
111:     def __init__(self, *args, extra_currents=None, ac_nodes=None,
112:                  extra_vsources=None, extra_elements=None, temp=27.0, **kw):
113:         super().__init__(*args, **kw)
114:         self.extra_currents = dict(extra_currents or {})
115:         self.ac_nodes = set(ac_nodes or ())
116:         self.extra_vsources = dict(extra_vsources or {})
117:         self.extra_elements = list(extra_elements or [])
118:         self.temp = temp
119:         self.supply_srcs = {}
120:         self.src_srcs = {}
121: 
122:     def _stimulus(self):
123:         lines = []
124:         for i, (node, val) in enumerate(self.supplies.items()):
125:             v = str(val)
126:             if node in self.ac_nodes:
127:                 v = v.rstrip() + " AC 1"
128:             lines.append(f"Vsupply{i} {node} 0 {v}")
129:             self.supply_srcs[node] = f"Vsupply{i}"
130:         for i, (node, val) in enumerate(self.sources.items()):
131:             v = str(val)
132:             if node in self.ac_nodes:
133:                 v = v.rstrip() + " AC 1"
134:             lines.append(f"Vsrc{i} {node} 0 {v}")
135:             self.src_srcs[node] = f"Vsrc{i}"
136:         for i, (node, val) in enumerate(self.extra_vsources.items()):
137:             lines.append(f"Vx{i} {node} 0 {val}")
138:         for node, spec in self.loads.items():
139:             lines.append(f"C_load_{node} {node} 0 {spec}")
140:         for node, val in self.extra_currents.items():
141:             lines.append(f"Ibias_{node} 0 {node} DC {val}")
142:         for ln in self.extra_elements:
143:             lines.append(ln)
144:         return lines
145: 
146:     def build_deck(self, analysis_lines, control):
147:         deck = super().build_deck(analysis_lines, control)
148:         out = []
149:         inserted = False
150:         for ln in deck.splitlines():
151:             out.append(ln)
152:             if ln.strip().startswith(".lib") and not inserted:
153:                 out.append(f".temp {self.temp}")
154:                 inserted = True
155:         return "\n".join(out)
156: 
157:     def custom(self, analysis, control, *, datfile=None, x_name="",
158:                probes=(), complex_data=False, analysis_lines=()):
159:         deck = self.build_deck(analysis_lines, control)
160:         r = run_spice(deck, workdir=self.workdir, timeout=self.timeout,
161:                       fmt="dat")
162:         res = SimResult(analysis=analysis, x_name=x_name,
163:                         returncode=r["returncode"],
164:                         stdout=r.get("stdout", ""), stderr=r.get("stderr", ""),
165:                         workdir=r.get("workdir", ""), deck=deck)
166:         _parse_print(res, probes)
167:         if datfile:
168:             for p in r.get("dat_paths") or []:
169:                 if os.path.basename(p) == datfile:
170:                     _parse_wrdata(res, p, x_name, probes, complex_data)
171:                     break
172:         return res
173: 
174: 
175: def parse_ports(netlist, cell):
176:     m = re.search(rf"^\.subckt\s+{re.escape(cell)}\s+(.+)$", netlist,
177:                   re.MULTILINE | re.IGNORECASE)
178:     if not m:
179:         raise ValueError(f".subckt {cell} not found in netlist")
180:     ports = m.group(1).split()
181:     roles = {"vdd": None, "vss": None, "inp": None, "inn": None,
182:              "out": None, "ibias": None}
183:     for p in ports:
184:         pl = p.lower()
185:         if pl in ("vdd", "vcc", "avdd"):
186:             roles["vdd"] = roles["vdd"] or p
187:         elif pl in ("vss", "gnd", "avss", "vss!"):
188:             roles["vss"] = roles["vss"] or p
189:         elif "ibias" in pl or "bias" in pl or pl in ("vb", "ib"):
190:             roles["ibias"] = roles["ibias"] or p
191:         elif pl in ("out", "vo", "o", "output"):
192:             roles["out"] = roles["out"] or p
193:         elif pl in ("inp", "ip", "vinp", "vip", "inp!"):
194:             roles["inp"] = roles["inp"] or p
195:         elif pl in ("inn", "inm", "im", "vinn", "vinm", "inm!"):
196:             roles["inn"] = roles["inn"] or p
197:         elif pl.startswith("in") and len(pl) >= 3:
198:             if pl[-1] in ("n", "m"):
199:                 roles["inn"] = roles["inn"] or p
200:             else:
201:                 roles["inp"] = roles["inp"] or p
202:         elif pl.startswith("vd"):
203:             roles["vdd"] = roles["vdd"] or p
204:         elif pl.startswith("vs"):
205:             roles["vss"] = roles["vss"] or p
206:     missing = [r for r, v in roles.items() if v is None]
207:     if missing:
208:         raise ValueError(f"could not identify port roles {missing} "
209:                          f"among {ports}")
210:     return roles
211: 
212: 
213: def _phase(res, name, f):
214:     re_v = res.signals.get(f"{name}.re")
215:     im_v = res.signals.get(f"{name}.im")
216:     if not re_v or not im_v:
217:         return None
218:     for fr, rv, iv in zip(res.x, re_v, im_v):
219:         if fr >= f:
220:             return math.degrees(math.atan2(iv, rv))
221:     return math.degrees(math.atan2(im_v[-1], re_v[-1]))
222: 
223: 
224: def _largest_contiguous(x, y, lo, hi):
225:     best = None
226:     cur = None
227:     for xi, yi in zip(x, y):
228:         if lo <= yi <= hi:
229:             if cur is None:
230:                 cur = [xi, xi]
231:             else:
232:                 cur[1] = xi
233:         else:
234:             if cur is not None:
235:                 if best is None or (cur[1] - cur[0]) > (best[1] - best[0]):
236:                     best = cur
237:                 cur = None
238:     if cur is not None and (best is None or (cur[1] - cur[0]) > (best[1] - best[0])):
239:         best = cur
240:     return (best[0], best[1]) if best else (None, None)
241: 
242: 
243: def _slew_segment(to, yo, t_start, t_end):
244:     """Slew rate over one edge via 10-90% crossings of the local swing."""
245:     xs, ys = [], []
246:     for t, y in zip(to, yo):
247:         if t_start <= t <= t_end:
248:             xs.append(t)
249:             ys.append(y)
250:     if len(xs) < 4:
251:         return None
252:     lo, hi = min(ys), max(ys)
253:     span = hi - lo
254:     if span <= 1e-9:
255:         return None
256: 
257:     def cross(level):
258:         for i in range(1, len(xs)):
259:             a, b = ys[i - 1], ys[i]
260:             if a < level <= b or a > level >= b:
261:                 if b == a:
262:                     return xs[i]
263:                 return xs[i - 1] + (level - a) / (b - a) * (xs[i] - xs[i - 1])
264:         return None
265: 
266:     t1 = cross(lo + 0.1 * span)
267:     t2 = cross(lo + 0.9 * span)
268:     if t1 is None or t2 is None:
269:         return None
270:     ta, tb = min(t1, t2), max(t1, t2)
271:     if tb <= ta:
272:         return None
273:     return 0.8 * span / (tb - ta)
274: 
275: 
276: def measure_all(netlist, cell, *, vdd=3.3, vss=0.0, vcm=1.65, ibias=20e-6,
277:                 cl=5e-12, corner="typical", temp=27.0, workdir="sim"):
278:     """Measure the full OTA metric set on any netlist (schematic or extracted).
279: 
280:     Returns an empty dict on any core-measurement failure so the flow treats
281:     it as a tool failure. Optional characterizations (CMRR/PSRR/ICMR/noise/
282:     offset) degrade to ``None`` rather than failing the run.
283:     """
284:     mcell = re.search(r"^\.subckt\s+(\S+)", netlist, re.MULTILINE | re.IGNORECASE)
285:     if mcell:
286:         cell = mcell.group(1)
287:     roles = parse_ports(netlist, cell)
288:     supplies = {roles["vdd"]: vdd, roles["vss"]: vss}
289:     sources = {roles["inp"]: f"DC {vcm}", roles["inn"]: f"DC {vcm}"}
290:     loads = {roles["out"]: f"{cl:.6g}"}
291:     extras = {roles["ibias"]: f"{ibias:.6g}"}
292:     out = roles["out"]
293:     inp = roles["inp"]
294:     wd = workdir
295:     os.makedirs(wd, exist_ok=True)
296: 
297:     try:
298:         # ── main run: op + ac + dc transfer + noise ────────────────────
299:         tb = OtaTB(netlist, cell, supplies=supplies,
300:                    sources={inp: f"DC {vcm}", roles["inn"]: f"DC {vcm}"},
301:                    loads=loads, corner=corner, temp=temp,
302:                    extra_currents=extras, ac_nodes={inp},
303:                    probes=[out], workdir=wd, timeout=600)
304:         vdd_src = "Vsupply%d" % list(supplies.keys()).index(roles["vdd"])
305:         inp_src = "Vsrc%d" % list(sources.keys()).index(inp)
306:         ctrl = [
307:             "op",
308:             f"print v({out}) i({vdd_src})",
309:             "ac dec 25 1 1e9",
310:             f"wrdata ac.dat v({out})",
311:             f"dc {inp_src} 0.4 2.9 0.005",
312:             f"wrdata dc.dat v({out})",
313:             "noise v(" + out + ") " + inp_src + " dec 10 1 100Meg",
314:             "print inoise_total onoise_total",
315:         ]
316:         res = tb.custom("main", ctrl, datfile="dc.dat", x_name="inp",
317:                         probes=[out])
318:         if not res.ok:
319:             return {}
320: 
321:         ac_res = tb.custom("ac", ["ac dec 25 1 1e9", f"wrdata ac2.dat v({out})"],
322:                            datfile="ac2.dat", x_name="freq", probes=[out],
323:                            complex_data=True)
324:         if not ac_res.ok or not ac_res.x:
325:             return {}
326:         mag = ac_res.get(out)
327:         if not mag or max(mag) <= 0:
328:             return {}
329:         db = [20.0 * math.log10(v) for v in mag]
330:         gain_db = db[0]
331:         bw_hz = None
332:         for f, d in zip(ac_res.x, db):
333:             if d <= gain_db - 3.0:
334:                 bw_hz = f
335:                 break
336:         ugf_hz = None
337:         for f, d in zip(ac_res.x, db):
338:             if d <= 0.0:
339:                 ugf_hz = f
340:                 break
341:         pm_deg = None
342:         if ugf_hz is not None:
343:             ph = _phase(ac_res, out, ugf_hz)
344:             if ph is not None:
345:                 pm_deg = 180.0 + ph
346: 
347:         m = {
348:             "gain_db": gain_db,
349:             "bw_hz": bw_hz if bw_hz is not None else float("nan"),
350:             "ugf_hz": ugf_hz if ugf_hz is not None else float("nan"),
351:             "pm_deg": pm_deg if pm_deg is not None else float("nan"),
352:         }
353: 
354:         # operating point
355:         vout_dc = res.value(f"v({out})")
356:         idd = abs(res.value(f"i({vdd_src})"))
357:         m["vout_dc"] = vout_dc
358:         m["idd_ua"] = idd * 1e6
359: 
360:         # DC transfer: swing + systematic offset
361:         voutc = res.get(out)
362:         m["vout_hi"] = max(voutc)
363:         m["vout_lo"] = min(voutc)
364:         off = None
365:         for i in range(1, len(voutc)):
366:             a, b = voutc[i - 1], voutc[i]
367:             if (a - 1.65) * (b - 1.65) <= 0 and b != a:
368:                 vin = res.x[i - 1] + (1.65 - a) / (b - a) * (res.x[i] - res.x[i - 1])
369:                 off = (vin - vcm) * 1000.0
370:                 break
371:         m["offset_mv"] = off
372: 
373:         # noise totals (input-/output-referred, integrated 1 Hz .. 100 MHz)
374:         m["inoise_rms"] = res.value("inoise_total")
375:         m["onoise_rms"] = res.value("onoise_total")
376:     except Exception as e:                      # noqa: BLE001
377:         print(f"[MEASURE] core measurement failed: {type(e).__name__}: {e}")
378:         return {}
379: 
380:     # ── common-mode AC (CMRR) ──────────────────────────────────────────
381:     try:
382:         tb_cm = OtaTB(netlist, cell, supplies=supplies,
383:                       sources={inp: f"DC {vcm}", roles["inn"]: f"DC {vcm}"},
384:                       loads=loads, corner=corner, temp=temp,
385:                       extra_currents=extras, ac_nodes={inp, roles["inn"]},
386:                       probes=[out], workdir=wd, timeout=600)
387:         cm = tb_cm.custom("ac", ["ac dec 15 1 1e7", f"wrdata cm.dat v({out})"],
388:                           datfile="cm.dat", x_name="freq", probes=[out],
389:                           complex_data=True)
390:         if cm.ok and cm.get(out):
391:             m["cmrr_db"] = gain_db - 20.0 * math.log10(cm.get(out)[0])
392:     except Exception:                           # noqa: BLE001
393:         pass
394: 
395:     # ── supply AC (PSRR) ───────────────────────────────────────────────
396:     try:
397:         tb_ps = OtaTB(netlist, cell,
398:                       supplies={roles["vdd"]: vdd, roles["vss"]: vss},
399:                       sources={inp: f"DC {vcm}", roles["inn"]: f"DC {vcm}"},
400:                       loads=loads, corner=corner, temp=temp,
401:                       extra_currents=extras, ac_nodes={roles["vdd"]},
402:                       probes=[out], workdir=wd, timeout=600)
403:         ps = tb_ps.custom("ac", ["ac dec 15 1 1e7", f"wrdata ps.dat v({out})"],
404:                           datfile="ps.dat", x_name="freq", probes=[out],
405:                           complex_data=True)
406:         if ps.ok and ps.get(out):
407:             m["psrr_db"] = gain_db - 20.0 * math.log10(ps.get(out)[0])
408:     except Exception:                           # noqa: BLE001
409:         pass
410: 
411:     # ── input common-mode range ────────────────────────────────────────
412:     try:
413:         # Both inputs are tied to one swept source, so the DUT sees a pure
414:         # common-mode sweep. The 0V tie source makes INN follow INP.
415:         tie = f"Vtie_cm {inp} {roles['inn']} 0"
416:         tb_ic = OtaTB(netlist, cell, supplies=supplies,

418:                       corner=corner, temp=temp, extra_currents=extras,
419:                       extra_elements=[tie], probes=[out], workdir=wd,

421:         ic = tb_ic.custom("dc", ["dc Vsrc0 0.4 3.0 0.05",

428:                                              ref - 0.5, ref + 0.5)
429:                 if lo is not None:
430:                     m["icmr_lo"] = lo
431:                     m["icmr_hi"] = hi
432:     except Exception:                           # noqa: BLE001
433:         pass
434: 
435:     # ── transient: slew rates + large-signal rails ─────────────────────
436:     try:
437:         tb_tr = OtaTB(netlist, cell, supplies=supplies,
438:                       sources={inp: "DC 1.35 PULSE(1.35 1.95 0 5n 5n 30u 60u)",

502:

169: def scale_device_widths(netlist: str, factor: float,
170:                         only: Optional[Sequence[str]] = None,
171:                         w_max: float = 10.0) -> str:
172:     """Multiply transistor widths by ``factor``.
173: 
174:     Respects the GF180 per-finger limit (W < 10 um): a tuner that produces an
175:     unbuildable device has not improved anything. ``only`` restricts the
176:     change to named instances, which is how post-layout tuning targets the
177:     devices on a parasitic-sensitive node instead of the whole circuit.
178:     """
179:     out = []
180:     for line in netlist.splitlines():
181:         m = _DEV.match(line)
182:         if not m:
183:             out.append(line)
184:             continue
185:         name = line.split()[0]
186:         if only and name not in only:
187:             out.append(line)
188:             continue
189:         unit = m.group("unit") or ""
190:         try:
191:             w = float(m.group("w"))
192:         except ValueError:
193:             out.append(line)
194:             continue
195:         scale = 1e6 if unit.lower().startswith("u") else 1.0
196:         new_w = w * factor
197:         if unit.lower().startswith("u") and new_w >= w_max:
198:             new_w = w_max - 0.01
199:         out.append(f"{m.group('head')}W={new_w:g}{unit}{m.group('tail')}")
200:     return "\n".join(out) + ("\n" if netlist.endswith("\n") else "")
201: 
202: 
203: def default_tune_pre(design: DesignPoint, report: SpecReport) -> DesignPoint:
204:     """Heuristic pre-layout sizing step.
205: 
206:     Low gain wants more device width; low bandwidth wants less capacitance,
207:     so it backs width off instead. When both fail the gain term wins, because
208:     a design that cannot reach its gain target has no working operating point
209:     to trade bandwidth against.
210:     """
211:     failing = {r.name for r in report.failures}
212:     factor = 1.0
213:     if "gain_db" in failing:
214:         factor = 1.3
215:     elif {"bw_hz", "ugf_hz"} & failing:
216:         factor = 0.8
217:     if factor == 1.0:
218:         return design
219: 
220:     step = int(design.circuit.get("_pre_step", 0)) + 1
221:     return design.evolve(
222:         netlist=scale_device_widths(design.netlist, factor),
223:         circuit={**design.circuit, "_pre_step": step, "w_factor": factor},
224:         note=f"pre_tune_{step}(w x{factor})")
225: 
226: 
227: def default_tune_post(design: DesignPoint, report: SpecReport,
228:                       degradation: Sequence[Degradation]) -> DesignPoint:
229:     """Heuristic PEX-aware step: circuit *and* layout constraints.
230: 
231:     Post-layout tuning is not only sizing. When a metric degrades from
232:     pre-layout to PEX the cause is parasitic — capacitance and resistance the
233:     schematic never had — so the response widens the critical nets and asks
234:     the placer to tighten the affected devices, alongside any sizing change.
235:     """
236:     worst = [d for d in degradation if d.worsened and d.status != "PASS"]
237:     failing = {r.name for r in report.failures} if report.results else set()
238: 
239:     layout = dict(design.layout)
240:     # Wider critical-net metal lowers series R; tighter placement lowers the
241:     # coupled length that produced the extra C in the first place.
242:     width = float(layout.get("critical_net_width", 0.28))
243:     layout["critical_net_width"] = round(min(width * 1.25, 1.0), 4)
244:     layout["tighten_matched_groups"] = True
245:     if worst:
246:         layout["parasitic_sensitive"] = [d.name for d in worst[:3]]
247: 
248:     factor = 1.0
249:     if "gain_db" in failing or any(d.name == "gain_db" for d in worst):
250:         factor = 1.2
251:     elif {"bw_hz", "ugf_hz"} & failing:
252:         factor = 0.9
253: 
254:     step = int(design.circuit.get("_pex_step", 0)) + 1
255:     netlist = (scale_device_widths(design.netlist, factor)
256:                if factor != 1.0 else design.netlist)
257:     return design.evolve(
258:         netlist=netlist, layout=layout,
259:         circuit={**design.circuit, "_pex_step": step},
260:         note=f"pex_tune_{step}(w x{factor}, net {layout['critical_net_width']}um)")
261: 
262: 
263: # ── branch-and-compare candidate evaluation ───────────────────────────
264: 
265: def make_candidate_proposer(*, specs, hooks_ref: Dict[str, object],
266:                             memory=None, strategy=None, archive=None,
267:                             verbosity: int = 1):
268:     """Build the ``propose_candidates`` hook: evaluate several edits, keep one.
269: 
270:     Each candidate is built and measured **independently from the same
271:     baseline**, in its own directory. That is what makes the result
272:     attributable: when bandwidth improves, exactly one change is responsible,
273:     and the losing branches are archived rather than silently folded in.
274: 
275:     Returns a callable matching :attr:`mbg.flow.FlowHooks.propose_candidates`.
276:     """
277:     from mbg.search import (SearchState, DesignMemory, CandidateResult,
278:                             ParetoArchive, default_strategy, select_best,
279:                             margin_of)
280:     from mbg.specs import evaluate_specs
281: 
282:     mem = memory if memory is not None else DesignMemory()
283:     strat = strategy or default_strategy()
284:     arch = archive if archive is not None else ParetoArchive()
285:     stagnation = {"n": 0}
286: 
287:     def _log(msg):
288:         if verbosity >= 1:
289:             print(msg)
290: 
291:     def propose(*, design, report, degradation, iteration, baseline_score,
292:                 budget):
293:         state = SearchState(design=design, report=report,
294:                             metrics={r.name: r.value for r in report.results
295:                                      if r.value is not None},
296:                             specs=specs, degradation=degradation, memory=mem,
297:                             iteration=iteration, phase="pex",
298:                             tier=1 + stagnation["n"])
299:         candidates = strat.propose(state, budget)
300:         if not candidates:
301:             _log("[SEARCH] no candidate moves left to try")
302:             return None, []
303: 
304:         _log(f"[SEARCH] evaluating {len(candidates)} candidate(s) against "
305:              f"baseline score {baseline_score:.4g}")
306:         results: List[CandidateResult] = []
307:         for c in candidates:
308:             # tag isolates this candidate's artifacts from every other branch
309:             c.design = c.design.evolve(
310:                 circuit={**c.design.circuit, "_tag": f"cand_{c.id}"})
311:             res = CandidateResult(candidate=c)
312:             try:
313:                 layout = hooks_ref["build_layout"](c.design)
314:                 if not layout.ok:
315:                     res.error = layout.message or "layout/verification failed"
316:                     res.decision = "ERROR"
317:                     _log(f"[SEARCH]   {c.id}: {res.error}")
318:                     results.append(res)
319:                     continue
320:                 metrics = hooks_ref["simulate_pex"](c.design, layout)
321:                 rep = evaluate_specs(metrics, specs, "pex")
322:                 res.ok, res.metrics, res.report = True, dict(metrics), rep
323:                 res.score, res.margin = rep.score, margin_of(rep)
324:                 res.artifacts = {"gds": layout.gds_path or "",
325:                                  "pex": layout.pex_netlist or ""}
326:                 res.candidate.params["_layout"] = layout
327:                 _log(f"[SEARCH]   {c.id}: score {res.score:.4g}"
328:                      + ("  PASSES ALL SPECS" if rep.passed else "")
329:                      + f"   ({c.change})")
330:             except Exception as e:                        # noqa: BLE001
331:                 res.error = f"{type(e).__name__}: {e}"
332:                 res.decision = "ERROR"
333:                 _log(f"[SEARCH]   {c.id}: ERROR {res.error}")
334:             results.append(res)
335:             arch.offer(res)
336: 
337:         winner, labelled = select_best(results, baseline_score)
338:         base_metrics = {r.name: r.value for r in report.results
339:                         if r.value is not None}
340:         for r in labelled:
341:             mem.record(r, baseline_score, base_metrics)
342: 
343:         records = [r.as_dict() for r in labelled]
344:         if winner is None:
345:             stagnation["n"] += 1
346:             _log("[SEARCH] no candidate beat the incumbent — escalating")
347:             return None, records
348:         stagnation["n"] = 0
349:         _log(f"[SEARCH] promoting {winner.candidate.id} "
350:              f"(score {baseline_score:.4g} -> {winner.score:.4g})")
351:         return winner.candidate.design, records
352: 
353:     propose.memory = mem          # exposed for reporting
354:     propose.archive = arch
355:     return propose
356: 
357: 
358: # ── hook factory ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────
359: 
360: def make_hooks(*, cell: str, out_node: str, in_node: str,
361:                supplies: Mapping[str, float],
362:                spec_names: Sequence[str] = (),
363:                bias: Optional[Mapping[str, str]] = None,
364:                outdir: Optional[str] = None,
365:                tune_pre=default_tune_pre,
366:                tune_post=default_tune_post,
367:                specs: Optional[Sequence] = None,
368:                branch: bool = True,
369:                verbosity: int = 0) -> FlowHooks:
370:     """Production hooks: real ngspice, real Magic, real netgen."""
371:     from mbg.pipeline import spice_to_gds_with_checks
372: 
373:     sim_kw = dict(out_node=out_node, in_node=in_node, supplies=supplies,
374:                   bias=bias)
375: 
376:     def _simulate_pre(design: DesignPoint) -> Mapping[str, float]:
377:         m = simulate_netlist(design.netlist, cell, spec_names, **sim_kw)
378:         if not m:
379:             raise RuntimeError(
380:                 "pre-layout simulation produced no usable data — check the "
381:                 "ngspice log; this is a tool failure, not a spec miss")
382:         return m
383: 
384:     def _build_layout(design: DesignPoint) -> LayoutResult:
385:         # Every build gets its own directory. The pipeline names artifacts
386:         # after the cell, so successive iterations used to overwrite each
387:         # other in place — which destroyed the best iteration's GDS and PEX
388:         # netlist the moment a later, worse one ran.
389:         tag = str(design.circuit.get("_tag") or design.note or "run")
390:         tag = re.sub(r"[^A-Za-z0-9_.-]+", "_", tag)[:40] or "run"
391:         wd = os.path.join(outdir or ".", "iterations", tag)
392:         os.makedirs(wd, exist_ok=True)
393:         prev = os.getcwd()
394:         try:
395:             os.chdir(wd)
396:             r = spice_to_gds_with_checks(
397:                 design.netlist, verbosity=verbosity,
398:                 router_config=router_config_from_layout(design.layout or {},
399:                                                         verbosity))
400:         except Exception as e:
401:             return LayoutResult(ok=False, failure=Failure.TOOL_FAILURE,
402:                                 message=f"layout generation failed: "
403:                                         f"{type(e).__name__}: {e}")
404:         finally:
405:             os.chdir(prev)
406:         drc = r.get("drc") or {}
407:         lvs = r.get("lvs") or {}
408:         pex = r.get("pex") or {}
409: 
410:         drc_ok = bool(drc.get("clean"))
411:         lvs_ok = bool(lvs.get("match"))
412:         # Verification gates extraction: LVS is not even consulted if DRC
413:         # failed, and PEX is not consulted if either did.
414:         out = LayoutResult(
415:             gds_path=r.get("gds_path"),
416:             drc=Outcome.PASS if drc_ok else Outcome.FAIL,
417:             lvs=(Outcome.PASS if lvs_ok else Outcome.FAIL) if drc_ok else Outcome.SKIP,
418:             raw=r)
419:         if not drc_ok:
420:             out.pex_extraction = Outcome.SKIP
421:             out.failure = Failure.VERIFICATION_FAILURE
422:             out.message = drc.get("summary", "DRC failed")
423:             return out
424:         if not lvs_ok:
425:             out.pex_extraction = Outcome.SKIP
426:             out.failure = Failure.VERIFICATION_FAILURE
427:             out.message = str((lvs.get("summary") or {}).get("message", "LVS failed"))
428:             return out
429: 
430:         pex_path = pex.get("pex_path") or pex.get("netlist_path")
431:         if not pex_path or not os.path.isfile(pex_path):
432:             out.pex_extraction = Outcome.FAIL
433:             out.failure = Failure.TOOL_FAILURE
434:             out.message = "PEX extraction produced no netlist"
435:             return out
436:         out.pex_extraction = Outcome.PASS
437:         out.pex_netlist = pex_path
438:         out.ok = True

90: class DesignPoint:
91:     """One candidate design: the netlist plus the knobs allowed to move.
92: 
93:     ``circuit`` and ``layout`` are kept apart on purpose. Post-layout tuning
94:     is not only transistor sizing — widening a parasitic-sensitive net or
95:     constraining a matched pair is a layout action, and an optimizer that can
96:     only resize devices cannot fix a routing-induced problem.
97:     """
98:     netlist: str = ""
99:     cell: str = ""
100:     circuit: Dict[str, object] = field(default_factory=dict)
101:     layout: Dict[str, object] = field(default_factory=dict)
102:     note: str = ""
103:

124:     """The real work, injected.

126:     Every hook may raise; the driver converts an exception into a
127:     TOOL_FAILURE rather than letting it look like a design problem.
128: 
129:     simulate_pre(design)            -> Mapping[str, float]   measured metrics

140:                                  DesignPoint]] = None

142:     #: designs to evaluate independently. When present the flow branches:

320: class DesignFlow:
321:     """Drives the two optimization loops."""
322: 
323:     def __init__(self, hooks: FlowHooks, config: FlowConfig):
324:         self.hooks = hooks
325:         self.cfg = config
326: 
327:     # -- diagnostics --
328:     def log(self, level: int, msg: str) -> None:
329:         if self.cfg.verbosity >= level:
330:             print(msg)
331: 
332:     def _specs_block(self, report: SpecReport) -> None:
333:         if self.cfg.verbosity >= 1:
334:             print("\n[SPECS]")
335:             print(format_spec_table(report))
336:             print("")
337: 
338:     # -- entry point --
339:     def run(self, design: DesignPoint) -> FlowResult:
340:         started = time.time()
341:         res = FlowResult(cell=design.cell)
342: 
343:         if not self.cfg.specs:
344:             res.status = Outcome.ERROR
345:             res.failure = Failure.CONFIGURATION_FAILURE
346:             res.message = ("no target specifications were given, so there is "
347:                            "nothing to optimise against. Pass FlowConfig("
348:                            "specs=[Spec(...), ...]).")
349:             res.elapsed = time.time() - started
350:             return res
351: 
352:         design = self._loop_pre_layout(design, res)
353:         if res.status in (Outcome.ERROR, Outcome.TIMEOUT):
354:             res.elapsed = time.time() - started
355:             self._finish(res)
356:             return res
357:         if res.failure == Failure.DESIGN_FAILURE and not (
358:                 res.best_pre and res.best_pre.passed):
359:             # Pre-layout never converged. Going to layout would burn EDA time
360:             # on a circuit already known to miss its targets.
361:             res.status = Outcome.NOT_CONVERGED
362:             res.stage = Stage.PRE_OPTIMIZATION
363:             res.elapsed = time.time() - started
364:             self._finish(res)
365:             return res
366: 
367:         if self.cfg.skip_layout:
368:             res.stage = Stage.PRE_EVALUATION
369:             res.status = Outcome.PASS if (res.best_pre and res.best_pre.passed) \
370:                 else Outcome.FAIL
371:             res.message = "skip_layout=True: stopped after pre-layout evaluation"
372:             res.elapsed = time.time() - started
373:             self._finish(res)
374:             return res
375: 
376:         self._loop_pex(design, res)
377:         res.elapsed = time.time() - started
378:         self._finish(res)
379:         return res
380: 
381:     # -- LOOP A --
382:     def _loop_pre_layout(self, design: DesignPoint, res: FlowResult) -> DesignPoint:
383:         best_score = float("inf")
384:         stagnant = 0
385: 
386:         for i in range(1, self.cfg.max_pre_iterations + 1):
387:             t0 = time.time()
388:             res.stage = Stage.PRE_SIMULATION
389:             self.log(1, f"[FLOW] Stage: {Stage.PRE_SIMULATION}")
390:             self.log(1, f"[FLOW] Pre-layout iteration: {i}/{self.cfg.max_pre_iterations}")
391: 
392:             try:
393:                 metrics = self.hooks.simulate_pre(design)
394:             except Exception as e:
395:                 rec = IterationRecord(phase="pre_layout", iteration=i,
396:                                       status=Outcome.ERROR,
397:                                       failure=Failure.TOOL_FAILURE,
398:                                       message=f"{type(e).__name__}: {e}",
399:                                       elapsed=time.time() - t0)
400:                 res.pre_layout.append(rec)
401:                 res.status, res.failure = Outcome.ERROR, Failure.TOOL_FAILURE
402:                 res.message = (f"pre-layout simulation failed to run: {e}. "
403:                                f"This is a tool problem, not a specification "
404:                                f"miss — no tuning was attempted.")
405:                 self.log(1, f"[ERROR][SIMULATION] {res.message}")
406:                 return design
407: 
408:             res.stage = Stage.PRE_EVALUATION
409:             report = evaluate_specs(metrics, self.cfg.specs, source="pre_layout")
410:             self._specs_block(report)
411: 
412:             rec = IterationRecord(
413:                 phase="pre_layout", iteration=i, spec_pass=report.passed,
414:                 score=round(report.score, 6),
415:                 status=Outcome.PASS if report.passed else Outcome.FAIL,
416:                 failure=Failure.NONE if report.passed else Failure.SPEC_FAILURE,
417:                 metrics=report.as_dict(), elapsed=time.time() - t0)
418:             res.pre_layout.append(rec)
419: 
420:             if report.score < best_score - self.cfg.min_improvement:
421:                 best_score, stagnant = report.score, 0
422:                 res.best_pre, res.best_pre_design = report, design
423:             else:
424:                 stagnant += 1
425:                 if res.best_pre is None:
426:                     res.best_pre, res.best_pre_design = report, design
427: 
428:             if report.passed:
429:                 self.log(1, "[FLOW] Pre-layout specifications satisfied.")
430:                 self.log(1, "[FLOW] Starting layout generation.")
431:                 res.failure = Failure.NONE
432:                 return res.best_pre_design or design
433: 
434:             if i >= self.cfg.max_pre_iterations:
435:                 res.failure = Failure.DESIGN_FAILURE
436:                 res.message = (f"pre-layout optimisation did not converge in "
437:                                f"{self.cfg.max_pre_iterations} iterations")
438:                 break
439:             if stagnant >= self.cfg.patience:

633:     def _advance(self, design, res, pre_report, degradation, i, report,
634:                  best_score):
635:         """Produce the next design, by branching when we can.
636: 
637:         With a ``propose_candidates`` hook the flow evaluates several distinct
638:         edits against the *same* baseline and keeps the measured winner. That
639:         is what makes credit assignment meaningful: each candidate is scored
640:         on its own, so an improvement is attributable to one change rather
641:         than shared out among everything applied that iteration.
642: 
643:         Without the hook it falls back to the single-edit tuner.
644:         """
645:         if self.hooks.propose_candidates is None:
646:             return self._tune_post(design, res, pre_report, degradation, i,
647:                                    current=report)
648:         try:
649:             picked, records = self.hooks.propose_candidates(
650:                 design=design, report=report, degradation=degradation,
651:                 iteration=i, baseline_score=report.score,
652:                 budget=self.cfg.candidates_per_iteration)
653:         except Exception as e:
654:             res.status, res.failure = Outcome.ERROR, Failure.TOOL_FAILURE
655:             res.message = f"candidate proposal raised {type(e).__name__}: {e}"
656:             return design
657:         res.candidates.extend(records or [])
658:         if picked is not None:
659:             return picked
660:         # Nothing beat the incumbent: fall back to the tuner so the search
661:         # still moves rather than stalling on an exhausted candidate set.
662:         return self._tune_post(design, res, pre_report, degradation, i,
663:                                current=report)
664: 
665:     def _tune_post(self, design, res, pre_report, degradation, i,
666:                    current: Optional[SpecReport] = None) -> DesignPoint:
667:         try:
668:             # The tuner is deciding what to change about THIS design, so it
669:             # needs this iteration's measurement. Passing the best-so-far
670:             # report meant the failing-metric set could describe a different
671:             # iteration than the design being modified.
672:             report = current if current is not None else (
673:                 res.best_pex or SpecReport())
674:             return self.hooks.tune_post(design, report, degradation)
675:         except Exception as e:
676:             res.status, res.failure = Outcome.ERROR, Failure.TOOL_FAILURE
677:             res.message = f"tune_post raised {type(e).__name__}: {e}"
678:             return design
679: 
680:     def _log_layout_block(self, layout: LayoutResult) -> None:
681:         if self.cfg.verbosity < 1:
682:             return
683:         print(f"[VERIFY] DRC {layout.drc}")
684:         print(f"[VERIFY] LVS {layout.lvs}")
685:         print(f"[PEX] Extraction {layout.pex_extraction}")
686:         if not layout.ok and layout.message:
687:             print(f"[PEX] downstream stages SKIPPED — {layout.message}")
688: 
689:     def _finish(self, res: FlowResult) -> None:
690:         if self.cfg.outdir:
691:             try:
692:                 res.write_history(os.path.join(self.cfg.outdir, "history.json"))
693:             except OSError:
694:                 pass
695:         if self.cfg.verbosity >= 1:
696:             print("")
697:             print(res.summary())

**Beta-multiplier reference (self-biased), MOS-only:**

- PMOS mirror P1=P2 sets equal currents.

Actually — here's a cleaner idea that is physically robust and simple to reason about, known as the **"pure MOS bandgap reference using a single diode-connected device biased by a PTAT current source"**:

1. **PTAT current source** (self-biased, MOS-only, no resistor): the classic **Widlar-like self-biased current mirror using a triode MOS** — but instead of triode MOS, use the **ΔVgs of two diode-connected MOSFETs** to generate a PTAT current. Configuration: 

```
VDD
 │
 P1 ──┐       P2 ──┐        (PMOS mirror, P2 = K·P1 or equal)
      │            │
      N1 ──┐      N2 ──┐
      (diode)  │       (diode)  │   N2 has larger W (lower Vgs)
             VSS      VSS
```

Here N1 and N2 are both diode-connected, at the same current I (mirror ratio 1:1), but N2 is wider (W2 = K·W1). Then Vgs2 < Vgs1, and the difference ΔV = Vgs1 - Vgs2 appears between the two branches. But the branches are independent — there's no feedback making a current. To make it self-biased (current determined by ΔV), we need the ΔV across a resistor. Without resistor, the mirror with *different* sizes creates a fixed current where the ΔVgs equals the headroom... 

The self-biased version: make the two branches have different *current* ratios (not same) and connect them so ΔVgs sets the current through a "resistor-like" MOS in triode. This is the beta-multiplier.

236:         self.mismatch = False

246:     # -- deck construction --

255:     def _core(self) -> str:
256:         """The subcircuit with any .lib line stripped — we add our own."""
257:         keep = [l for l in self.netlist.splitlines()

259:         return "\n".join(keep).strip()

261:     def _stimulus(self) -> List[str]:

264:             lines.append(f"V{'supply%d' % i} {node} 0 {val}")

266:             if isinstance(val, str):                 # a full source expression
267:                 lines.append(f"Vsrc{i} {node} 0 {val}")
268:             else:
269:                 lines.append(f"Vsrc{i} {node} 0 {val}")
270:         for node, spec in self.loads.items():
271:             lines.append(f"C_load_{node} {node} 0 {spec}")
272:         return lines
273: 
274:     def build_deck(self, analysis_lines: Sequence[str], control: Sequence[str]) -> str:
275:         """Assemble a complete, runnable ngspice deck."""
276:         if not os.path.isfile(self.pdk_models):
277:             raise FileNotFoundError(
278:                 f"PDK models not found at {self.pdk_models} — set PDK_ROOT/PDKPATH")
279:         head = []
280:         if os.path.isfile(self.pdk_design):
281:             # must come before the model library or the models fail to evaluate
282:             head.append(f".include '{self.pdk_design}'")
283:         head.append(f".lib '{self.pdk_models}' {self.corner}")
284:         if self.mismatch:
285:             head.append(".param sw_stat_mismatch=1")
286: 
287:         inst = f"Xdut {' '.join(self.ports)} {self.cell}"
288:         body = head + ["", self._core(), ""] + self._stimulus() + [inst, ""]
289:         body += list(analysis_lines)
290:         pre = [f"set seed={self.seed}"] if self.seed is not None else []
291:         body += [".control"] + pre + list(control) + [".endc", ".end", ""]
292:         return "\n".join(body)

294:     # -- running --

296:              probes: Sequence[str], workdir: Optional[str] = None,
297:              datfile: Optional[str] = None,

300:         res = SimResult(analysis=analysis, x_name=x_name, returncode=r["returncode"],
301:                         stdout=r.get("stdout", ""), stderr=r.get("stderr", ""),
302:                         workdir=r.get("workdir", ""), deck=deck)
303:         _parse_print(res, probes)
304:         paths = list(r.get("dat_paths") or [])
305:         # Read the file THIS analysis wrote. Testbenches reuse one workdir, so
306:         # ac.dat/dc.dat/tran.dat pile up side by side and taking the first
307:         # path silently returned a previous analysis's data — the transient
308:         # came back holding the DC sweep, with the output node missing.
309:         if datfile:

313:         for path in paths:

315:             break
316:         return res
