318:     # -- analyses --
319:     def op(self) -> SimResult:
320:         """Operating point: one value per probe."""
321:         probes = self.probes or self.ports
322:         ctrl = ["op"] + [f"print v({p})" for p in probes]
323:         return self._run("op", self.build_deck([], ctrl), "", probes)
324: 
325:     def dc(self, source_node: str, start: float, stop: float, step: float) -> SimResult:
326:         """Sweep a source and record the probes."""
327:         idx = list(self.sources).index(source_node) if source_node in self.sources else None
328:         vname = f"Vsrc{idx}" if idx is not None else source_node
329:         # Default to every port, not just the swept source: recording only
330:         # the stimulus tells you what you already know and leaves the
331:         # response — the reason for the sweep — out of the result.
332:         probes = self.probes or self.ports or [source_node]
333:         ctrl = [f"dc {vname} {start} {stop} {step}",
334:                 f"wrdata dc.dat {' '.join('v(%s)' % p for p in probes)}"]
335:         return self._run("dc", self.build_deck([], ctrl), "v_sweep", probes,
336:                          datfile="dc.dat")
337: 
338:     def ac(self, fstart: float, fstop: float, points: int = 20,
339:            variation: str = "dec", ac_node: Optional[str] = None) -> SimResult:
340:         """AC sweep. The stimulus node is given an AC magnitude of 1."""
341:         node = ac_node or (list(self.sources)[0] if self.sources else None)
342:         if node is None:
343:             raise ValueError("ac() needs a source node to excite")
344:         probes = self.probes or self.ports or [node]
345:         deck = self.build_deck([], [f"ac {variation} {points} {fstart} {fstop}",
346:                                     f"wrdata ac.dat {' '.join('v(%s)' % p for p in probes)}"])
347:         # Give the chosen source an AC magnitude of 1. The old pattern only
348:         # matched a single-token value, so a source written "DC 1.2" kept an
349:         # AC magnitude of zero and the sweep came back all zeros — a silent
350:         # wrong answer. Match the line, not the value, and refuse to run if
351:         # the source is not there at all.
352:         idx = list(self.sources).index(node)
353:         deck, n = re.subn(rf"^(Vsrc{idx}\s+{re.escape(node)}\s+0\s+.*)$",
354:                           r"\1 AC 1", deck, flags=re.MULTILINE)
355:         if n != 1:
356:             raise RuntimeError(
357:                 f"ac(): could not attach an AC magnitude to source "
358:                 f"'Vsrc{idx}' on node '{node}'. Without it every result is "
359:                 f"zero, so this fails instead of returning one.")
360:         return self._run("ac", deck, "frequency", probes, datfile="ac.dat",
361:                          complex_data=True)
362: 
363:     def tran(self, step: str, stop: str, start: str = "0") -> SimResult:
364:         """Transient analysis."""

371:     def monte_carlo(self, analysis: str = "op", runs: int = 25,
372:                     corner: str = "statistical", mismatch: bool = True,
373:                     **kwargs) -> MonteCarloResult:
374:         """Repeat an analysis with fresh device-mismatch draws.
375: 
376:         GF180 expresses mismatch as `delvto = mis_vth * sw_stat_mismatch`, so
377:         two things are needed for the samples to actually differ: a corner that
378:         carries the statistical parameters, and sw_stat_mismatch enabled. Just
379:         re-seeding the typical corner returns the same number every run — the
380:         mismatch term is multiplied by zero.
381:         """
382:         out = MonteCarloResult(measure=analysis)
383:         fn = {"op": self.op, "dc": self.dc, "ac": self.ac, "tran": self.tran}[analysis]
384:         base_wd, base_corner, base_mm, base_seed = (
385:             self.workdir, self.corner, self.mismatch, self.seed)
386:         self.corner, self.mismatch = corner, mismatch
387:         try:
388:             for i in range(runs):
389:                 self.workdir = os.path.join(base_wd, f"mc{i:03d}")
390:                 self.seed = i + 1
391:                 out.runs.append(fn(**kwargs))
392:         finally:
393:             self.workdir, self.corner = base_wd, base_corner
394:             self.mismatch, self.seed = base_mm, base_seed
395:         return out
396:

403:                          re.MULTILINE):
404:         res.scalars[m.group(1).lower()] = float(m.group(2))

1: """
2: @Owner: Huda

7: import os, re, subprocess, tempfile, shutil, csv, struct

10: # ── PDK path resolution ──────────────────────────────────
11: def pdk_path(subpath=""):
12:     pdk = os.environ.get("PDK", "gf180mcuD")
13:     for base in [
14:         os.environ.get("PDKPATH"),

26: # ── Run ngspice ──────────────────────────────────────────
27: def run_spice(netlist_content, *, workdir=None, timeout=300, fmt="raw"):
28:     """Run any ngspice netlist.
29: 
30:     Args:
31:         netlist_content: Full SPICE netlist as string.
32:         workdir: If None, uses temp dir (auto-cleaned).
33:         timeout: Max seconds.
34:         fmt: 'raw' for binary, 'dat' for wrdata text, or 'both'.
35:

37:         {"stdout": str, "stderr": str, "returncode": int,

39:          "workdir": str}
40:     """
41:     wd = os.path.abspath(workdir) if workdir else tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix="ngspice_")
42:     os.makedirs(wd, exist_ok=True)
43:     # Absolute: the subprocess runs with cwd=wd, so a relative path here made
44:     # ngspice look for <wd>/<wd>/input.spice and fail with "No such file".
45:     inp = os.path.join(wd, "input.spice")
46:     with open(inp, "w") as f:
47:         f.write(netlist_content)
48: 
49:     try:
50:         r = subprocess.run(["ngspice", "-b", inp], capture_output=True, text=True,
51:                            timeout=timeout, cwd=wd)
52:     except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
53:         print(f"[TIMEOUT] ngspice timed out after {timeout}s — workdir kept: {wd}")
54:         return {"stdout": "", "stderr": "", "returncode": -1,

59:     dat_paths = []

62:             raw_path = os.path.join(wd, f)
63:         elif f.endswith(".dat"):
64:             dat_paths.append(os.path.join(wd, f))
65: 
66:     if r.returncode != 0:
67:         print(f"[WARN] ngspice returned {r.returncode} — workdir kept: {wd}")
68: 
69:     return {
70:         "stdout": r.stdout,
71:         "stderr": r.stderr,
72:         "returncode": r.returncode,

## Assistant (Build · DeepSeek V4 Flash · 90.1s)

_Thinking:_

I now understand the measurement infrastructure. Now, before writing the full flow script, let me first do a **design exploration in ngspice directly** to find a working topology. I'll write a quick exploration script that:

1. Tests my candidate topology with ngspice directly (via a temporary deck).
2. Sweeps temp (-40..125), VDD (2.7..3.6), IBIAS (10/20/30uA).
3. Reports VREF, tempco, line regulation, IDD.

This is the cheapest way to converge on a circuit before committing to the full flow.

**Let me design my candidate topology now — the "two-branch, IBIAS-fed, self-cascode (SCM) subthreshold-PTAT + strong-inversion CTAT" reference.**

Let me define it concretely. I want:

1. A PTAT current generator driven from IBIAS. 
2. A CTAT diode that produces Vgs with negative tempco.
3. A summation so VREF ≈ 1.2V with flat tempco.

Let me think about the SCM PTAT more carefully, since it's the crux.

**SCM (self-cascode MOSFET) PTAT generator** — the classic resistorless PTAT:

Consider this circuit:
```
VDD
 │
 P1 ─── node A ─── N1 (diode-connected, W/L = (W/L)1) ─── VSS
 │
 P2 ─── node B ─── N2 ─── node C ─── N3 (diode-connected, W/L = (W/L)3) ─── VSS
```
P1 = P2 (equal W). N1 diode: Vgs1 = V_A. 
N3 diode (bottom of right branch): Vgs3 = V_C.
N2 (top of right branch): gate tied to V_C (same as N3's gate), source at V_C, drain at V_B. N2 and N3 are a **self-cascode pair**: same current I through both, same Vgs (both gate tied to V_C... wait N2's gate = V_C, N3's gate = V_C, both sources at V_C and VSS respectively). N2: Vgs = V_C - V_C = 0?? No.

Let me redo: N3's gate and drain are at node C. N2's gate is at node C. N2's source is at node C. So N2 has Vgs = 0. That's off. Wrong configuration.

The self-cascode: N2's *source* is at node C (above N3's drain which is node C). N2's gate is at V_A (the left branch's diode node) or at its own... The standard SCM: two devices in series, top device's gate tied to bottom's drain? Let me get it right.

**Self-cascode MOSFET (SCM)**: M_bottom diode-connected (gate=drain=node X), M_top in series above, M_top's gate tied to node X as well. Then M_top has Vgs = V_X - V_src(M_top) = V_X - V_X = 0. No — if M_top's source is at node X (which is M_bottom's drain), then Vgs(top) = 0. Wrong again.

OK the correct SCM: M_bottom diode-connected: gate=drain=X, source=GND. M_top: source=X, drain=Y, gate=GND?? No.

The SCM is:
- M1 (bottom): gate = X, drain = X, source = GND. (diode)
- M2 (top): gate = X, drain = Y, source = X.  → Vgs2 = X - X = 0. Still 0!

Hmm. I misremember. The "self-cascode" / "cascode current source without extra bias": 

M2 top, gate tied to VDD, source at X, drain at Y. M1 bottom diode at X. Then M2's Vgs = VDD - X. M2 operates with whatever Vgs makes it pass I = current of M1. Since M1 diode sets V_X = Vgs1(I), M2's Vgs = VDD - Vgs1, which is large. Not standard.

Let me look at the actual SCM current reference (from literature, e.g. "A CMOS voltage reference" by Kok):

The SCM current source (classic, from Filanovsky / Vittoz):
```
VDD
 │
 P1 ── node1 ── N_a (diode) ── VSS
 P2 ── node2 ── N_b (top, gate=node1) ── node1' ... 
```

I'm clearly fuzzy on this. Rather than risk a mis-configured subthreshold generator, let me choose a **fully robust, well-understood topology that I can derive by hand**: 

**THE PLAN — "Current-mirror + ΔVgs PTAT via subthreshold diode + CTAT diode, summed through ratioed current mirrors into a diode-connected output" is too fancy.**

Let me instead use a topology I can 100% derive and simulate in a couple of iterations:

**Topology: "IBIAS-mirrored dual-diode reference with temperature-programmed current density"**

```
VDD
 │
 IBIAS ─── node bias ─── Mb (nfet diode) ─── VSS     (sets Vgs_bias)
 │
 Mp1 ─── node A ─── M1 (nfet diode, W1) ─── VSS      (I1 = k1·IBIAS)
 │
 Mp2 ─── node B ─── M2 (nfet diode, W2 > W1) ─── VSS  (I2 = k2·IBIAS)
```

Then VREF = Vgs1 (CTAT at constant current) is ~0.8V with -1.7mV/°C. The compensation: ΔVgs = Vgs1 - Vgs2 is PTAT-ish at T^0.75 — too weak as computed.

The compensation needs a *strong* PTAT. The ONLY strong resistorless PTAT is subthreshold ΔVgs = n·VT·ln(ratio). n·VT = 1.5·26mV = 39mV, ln(ratio) max maybe ln(100)≈4.6 → 180mV, tempco +0.6mV/°C per 180mV → need ~300mV ΔVgs to cancel 1.7mV/°C... ln(ratio) = 1.7·T/n/VT... at T=300: 1.7e-3·300/(1.5·26e-3) = 13 → ratio = e^13 = 442000. Not feasible.

Hmm wait, that math says subthreshold ΔVgs alone can't reach the needed PTAT slope either with feasible ratios (n·VT·ln(r) slope = (n·VT/T)·ln(r) = 39e-3/300·ln(r) V/°C = 0.13e-3·ln(r)). For slope 1.7e-3 V/°C: ln(r) = 13. Yes, e^13 too big. BUT we don't need to cancel the whole -1.7 mV/°C — we need to cancel it down to ≤ 0.36 mV/°C. So need net +1.4 mV/°C from PTAT. Still ln(r) ≈ 10.8. Too big.

**So the linear-summation approach needs amplification of the PTAT voltage.** In MOS-only, amplification = current mirror ratio (current gain) + transimpedance... The standard technique in MOS-only references:

**The PTAT voltage is generated across a device (diode-connected nfet in subthreshold, Vgs = Vth + n·VT·ln(...)). Then a PTAT *current* is derived I_PTAT = (n·VT·ln(r))/R, and with R from a triode MOS this is messy.**

OK here's the cleanest known approach that ACTUALLY works and is used in real CMOS-only references: **use the CTAT diode's Vgs, and make the *current through it* PTAT enough that its Vgs tempco flattens.** Recall my earlier analysis: Vgs = Vth + Vod where Vod = √(2·I/β), and if I ∝ T^α, dVod/dT = ((α+1.5)/2)·Vod/T. For near-zero net tempco: need ((α+1.5)/2)·Vod/T = |dVth/dT| ≈ 1.5 mV/°C. If α=1 (true PTAT current): Vod = 1.5e-3·300/1.25 = 0.36V. So with Vod ≈ 0.36V, VREF = Vth+Vod ≈ 1.06-1.1V, and tempco ≈ 0. If α is a bit more than 1, Vod needed is smaller. So **I need a true-PTAT current source (I ∝ T^1) and a diode-connected device with Vod ≈ 0.35V.** That's buildable!

Now, can I make a **true PTAT current** in MOS-only, resistorless, robustly? 

The **subthreshold ΔVgs current reference (Vittoz 2T)** is the canonical answer but fragile. There's a **stronger, more robust alternative: the "CMOS PTAT current source" using two diode-connected devices at different current densities where the ΔVgs develops across a triode-MOS resistor** — this is a "MOS bandgap" with the resistor as a triode MOS. The current I ∝ ΔVgs/R_triode. With ΔVgs PTAT (n·VT·ln(r)) and R_triode ∝ T^1.5 (negative tempco of μ → R up with T)... I ∝ T/T^1.5 = T^-0.5. Not PTAT. Bad.

Actually, let me search for the correct SCM PTAT circuit from memory of the literature (Filanovsky & Allam, "Mutual compensation of mobility and threshold voltage temperature effects with applications in CMOS circuits", and the "voltage reference" circuits).

The classic MOS-only PTAT current reference (no resistor) is the **"self-cascode MOSFET (SCM) current reference"** — correct topology:

```
         VDD
          │
         P1 ── X1
          │
         P2 ── X2 ── M2 (nfet) ── X3
          │              │
         VSS        M1 (nfet, diode) ── VSS
```
Where M1: gate=drain=X3, source=VSS. M2: gate=X3, drain=X2, source=X3. Wait that gives Vgs(M2)=0 again. 

Hmm. Let me think about what makes a self-cascode PTAT:
The trick (from the SCM current source) is that M2 and M1 share gate voltage, M2 in triode-ish, and:
- M1: diode, W1, L1. 
- M2: same W, same L, gate tied to X3 (M1's gate/drain), drain at X2, source at X3.
So Vgs2 = V(X3) - V(X3) = 0. That's definitely wrong.

Alternative SCM: **M2's gate tied to VDD** (i.e., M2 = "gate-at-VDD" cascode):
- M1: diode-connected nfet, gate=drain=X3, source=VSS.
- M2: nfet, gate=VDD, drain=X2, source=X3.
Then M2 conducts with Vgs2 = VDD - X3. Current through M2 = M1's current (series). M2 is deeply on (Vgs2 large). Not PTAT.

The actual PTAT SCM from the literature ("Kok, ... , CMOS temperature sensor / reference"):

I recall now: the SCM-PTAT (also called the "2T-PTAT current source" by Vittoz):
```
M1: nfet, diode-connected (gate=drain=A), source=GND.  W1, L
M2: nfet, gate=A (tied to M1 gate), drain=B, source=GND.  W2 = K·W1
```
A current I flows into node A (from a mirror). M1 and M2 have the SAME Vgs = V_A, but different W. Both try to conduct I·(W/ΣW) according to their widths: M2 with K×W1 conducts K× more at the same Vgs. In subthreshold, V_A is set by total current density. The classic PTAT: if I1:I2 = 1:K are forced (by two mirror transistors), the ΔVgs = Vgs1 - Vgs2 = n·VT·ln(K) appears... but you need the two *different* Vgs nodes.

**I think the cleanest correct subthreshold PTAT is the one where two diode-connected nfets carry DIFFERENT currents (ratio K) but are forced to have the same drain voltage, and the difference Vgs1 - Vgs2 = n·VT·ln(K·(W2/W1)) appears as a PTAT voltage across two mirror devices.** Then to get a PTAT *current*, run that PTAT voltage across the "resistor" of a triode MOS.

So the fully-workable circuit is:
```
VDD
 │
 P1 ── nodeA ── M1 (nfet diode, W1) ── VSS      I1 = I
 │
 P2 ── nodeB ── M2 (nfet diode, W2) ── VSS      I2 = K·I  (mirror P2=K·P1)
```
Vgs1 - Vgs2 = n·VT·ln(K·W2/W1) → PTAT voltage ΔV. This ΔV is between nodeA and nodeB... but there's no current path between them. So this alone generates a PTAT *voltage difference*, not a current.

To make a current, we force this ΔV across a device. **The beta-multiplier does exactly this** with a resistor. MOS-only version: put a triode NMOS between the two sources/drains.

Actually here's the classic **all-MOS "MOSFET-only bandgap"** (as in the prior repo example and literature): the beta-multiplier where the "R" is a triode MOS. The prior repo design did exactly that (XM_r in triode). The issue is tempco of that current.

Hmm, this doesn't yet sum CTAT+PTAT properly. The output VREF = Vgs5 (a diode). To compensate, Vgs5 must be biased with a temperature-shaped current.

Let me think about **the current-driven compensation** once more, concretely:

If VREF = Vgs of a diode-connected nfet M5, and I5 is the current through it, then:
VREF(T) = Vth(T) + √(2·I5(T)/β5(T))
= Vth0 - a·(T-T0) + √(2·I5(T)·μ0·Cox·(T/T0)^1.5·.../ (W/L))

If I5 is proportional to T^α:
Vod(T) ∝ T^{(α+1.5)/2}
d(Vod)/dT = ((α+1.5)/2)·Vod/T0 at T0.

We need: -a + ((α+1.5)/2)·Vod/T0 ≈ 0.
With a ≈ 1.4-1.6 mV/°C and T0=300: Vod ≈ a·T0·2/(α+1.5). For α=1: Vod = 1.5e-3·300·2/2.5 = 0.36V.
So: **VREF = Vth + 0.36V ≈ 1.06-1.1V.** To hit 1.2V, we need Vth+Vod=1.2 → Vod=0.5 (if Vth=0.7), requiring a = 1.5e-3 → (α+1.5)/2·0.5/300 = 1.5e-3 → α+1.5 = 1.8 → α = 0.3. So a weaker-than-PTAT current (α≈0.3) with Vod=0.5V. Or Vth=0.6 (thicker/well device) with Vod=0.6, α≈0. Or simply accept VREF≈1.1V and... no, must be 1.15-1.25.

So the required α (temperature exponent of the output current) is smallish (0-1). We can also stack two diodes (VREF = VgsA + VgsB ≈ 1.6V, too high) — or use Vgs + a fraction. Or use a PMOS diode with slightly different Vth.

**Key insight: I need a current source whose temperature exponent α I can tune between ~0 and ~1.** The natural tuning knob: the PTAT/CTAT balance in a self-biased beta-multiplier, or simply: mirror the IBIAS (assumed constant-T, α=0) — then α=0, Vod must satisfy -a + 0.75·Vod/T0 = 0 → Vod = a·T0/0.75 = 1.5e-3·300/0.75 = 0.6V. With Vth=0.7 → VREF = 1.3V. Slightly high; with Vth 0.65 → 1.25. Hmm, that's within allowed 1.15-1.25! 

So **even a plain constant-current (α=0) diode-connected nfet with Vod≈0.5-0.6V** could hit VREF≈1.15-1.25V with tempco near zero IF the Vod is large enough. Let me compute tempco precisely:
- dVth/dT: for gf180 3.3V nfet, ~ -1.3 to -1.8 mV/°C. Need exact.
- Vod = √(2·I·L/(μ0·Cox·W·(T/T0)^1.5)). Vod(T) = Vod0·(T/T0)^0.75. dVod/dT = 0.75·Vod0·(1/300) = 0.0025·Vod0 V/°C. For Vod0=0.6: +1.5 mV/°C. For -1.5 mV/°C Vth: net ≈ 0! 

So **a single diode-connected nfet biased at constant current with Vod ≈ 0.55-0.65V can give near-zero tempco at 27°C.** The tempco is only "flat" near 27°C though — the curve is concave (dVgs/dT = -a + 0.75·Vod(T)/T changes with T: at low T, μ higher → Vod lower → less positive → net more negative; at high T, more positive). This curvature causes residual error, but within ±0.36 mV/°C average it might pass 300ppm/°C. Let me just simulate.

But wait — Vod=0.6V with μ·Cox ≈ 200 µA/V² (gf180 nfet), I=20µA: (W/L) = 2I/(μCox·Vod²) = 2·20e-6/(200e-6·0.36) = 40/72 ≈ 0.56. So W/L ≈ 0.56, e.g., W=1.12µ, L=2µ. Fine. But such a device has low gm, high sensitivity to IBIAS (VREF sensitivity = 1/gm·ΔI...). IBIAS sensitivity: dVgs/dI = 1/gm. gm = √(2·μCox·(W/L)·I) = √(2·200e-6·0.56·20e-6) = √(4.5e-9) ≈ 67µS. dV/dI = 1/67µS = 15 mV/µA. Over 10-30µA IBIAS: ±10µA → ±150mV. That's a huge VREF change over IBIAS range — probably unacceptable ("characterize" — no explicit limit, but VREF must stay 1.15-1.25 in nominal; characterization reports it). Actually a ±150mV swing for ±10µA is bad and will likely be flagged by reviewers.

To reduce IBIAS sensitivity, the reference should be **self-biased** (current set by the ΔVgs core, not by IBIAS directly), with IBIAS merely *starting* the circuit. The classic self-biased reference: IBIAS seeds a diode-connected device that sets a voltage, which drives a self-biased beta-multiplier core that regeneratively sets its own current; IBIAS only needs to be above the minimum to ensure startup. That gives much better IBIAS rejection.

So the topology should be **self-biased with a startup current from IBIAS**.

The main challenge: line regulation ≤ 100 mV/V (i.e., ≤ 60 mV over 0.6V supply change) requires a good supply-independent bias. The prior design had 304 mV/V — poor. A self-biased reference with a proper PMOS mirror and cascoding can get line reg down.

For the VREF variation over 3.0-3.6V ≤ 60mV: need the reference current to be supply-independent. A self-biased core achieves this because both branches track supply through the mirror; the diode-connected CTAT VREF node then has good line rejection as long as the PMOS mirror has high output impedance (cascode helps) and there's no direct supply feedthrough to the VREF node.

Let me design **a self-biased, cascoded PMOS-mirror MOS-only reference**:

```
VDD
 │        │        │
 P1a ─ P1b  P2a ─ P2b  P3a ─ P3b     (cascoded PMOS mirrors)
 │        │        │
va        vb      vref
 │        │        │
M1 (diode) M2 (diode) M4 (output diode nfet)
 │        │        │
VSS      VSS      VSS
```

For self-biasing, the current is set by the ΔVgs between M1 and M2 (different W). But as computed, without a resistor the mirror current isn't uniquely set... Actually here's the self-biased structure that works WITHOUT a resistor (the classic "self-biased Widlar-like mirror using ΔVgs directly"):

Consider the **self-biased current source using two diode-connected devices in a current-mirror loop**:
```
VDD
 │             │
 P1 ── nodeA ── M1(diode,W1) ── VSS
 P2 ── nodeB ── M2(diode,W2) ── VSS
```
P1 and P2 mirror with ratio, say P2 = K·P1 (I2 = K·I1). Then Vgs1 - Vgs2 = ΔVgs. But where's the feedback? The loop is: nodeA drives P1&P2 gates. Currents I1,I2 determined by P sizes. This is just a forced-ratio arrangement (like the OTA bias); no self-regulating element. The absolute current is set by the mirror gate voltage, which is set by M1's diode: I1 = I_beta(Vgs1)... the mirror ratio determines I2 = K·I1. For this to be self-consistent (both P1,P2 at same Vgs since shared gate), we need... they're at the same Vgs (shared gate node). P1, P2 same Vgs, different W → I2/I1 = K. OK so I2=K·I1 forced by mirror. Then M2 carries K·I1 at Vgs2 = Vgs1 - ΔV. But M2's gate is nodeB, M2's Vgs = Vgs2 = V(nodeB). For M2 to conduct K·I1 with width W2 at Vgs2... this just sets the operating point, consistent. There's no feedback forcing a unique current. I1 is set by the gate voltage V(nodeA) which the diode M1 sets = Vgs1(I1). And I1 = I_beta(Vgs1) — the diode equation — always satisfied. So ANY I1 works? No: P1's current is I1, and M1's diode current must equal I1 (KCL at nodeA). M1 diode passes current as function of Vgs1. Vgs1 is the gate voltage of P1 (nodeA) which is set by... the whole loop. This has a continuum of solutions unless there's a degenerating element. Without a resistor, the self-biased mirror is degenerate (the classic "beta-multiplier needs a resistor to fix the current"). 

That's why ALL MOS-only self-biased references use a triode MOS as the degenerating "resistor". The prior repo design did. There's no way around needing a resistive-like element for current *setting* (self-bias). Using a triode MOSFET as that element is the standard, legitimate MOS-only approach (the device is a MOSFET, not an "explicit resistor").

So structurally my design will also have a triode-MOS degenerating element — that's unavoidable for a self-biased reference. But I can make the arrangement, the output stage, and the compensation different from the repo example so it's not a copy. Wait — this is getting complicated with the cascode wiring and I haven't verified the topology is even correct. Let me carefully define a **simple, correct beta-multiplier** first (verify by simulation), then improve.

**Simple beta-multiplier (my own layout of it), verified topology:**

```
VDD
 │        │
 P1 ── na ── M1(diode) ── VSS      (leg 1)
 P2 ── nb ── M2 ── X ── M3(triode R) ── VSS   (leg 2)
```
- P1, P2 equal PMOS mirror (shared gate at node Pg).
- P1's gate = P2's gate = node Pg, which is tied to na (leg1 top) — diode-connected PMOS P1.
- M1: nfet diode-connected at na.
- M2: nfet, gate = na, source = X.
- M3: nfet triode, gate = VDD, drain = X, source = VSS. (the "resistor")
- Mirror ratio: P2 = K·P1 (W2 = K·W1). Then I2 = K·I1.
- M1 vs M2: M2 has W2n = K·W1n too? The classic: M1 and M2 have the same W/L, so M2 wants K× current at same Vgs; but M2's source is at X (V_X > 0), so M2's Vgs = V(na) - V(X) < V(na) = Vgs1. The voltage across M3 (triode R) = V_X = Vgs1 - Vgs2 = ΔVgs.
- ΔVgs = V_X = I2·R3 → I2 = ΔVgs/R3. Self-biased: I2 determined by ΔVgs (via W ratio K and current ratio) and R3. This gives a unique, supply-independent current (to first order).

The current: I2 = K·I1; ΔVgs = Vgs1 - Vgs2 with Vgs2 = Vgs1 - ΔVgs. In strong inversion:
I1 = ½·β1·(Vgs1-Vth)², I2 = ½·β2·(Vgs2-Vth)², β2 = K·β1 (M2 K× wider).
→ Vgs1-Vth = √(2I1/β1), Vgs2-Vth = √(2I2/β2) = √(2K·I1/(K·β1)) = √(2I1/β1) = Vgs1-Vth. 
So Vgs2 = Vgs1 → ΔVgs = 0?? That's the degenerate case when M2 has the same current density as M1 (both K× scaled). ΔVgs = V_X = I2·R3 = 0 → no current! 

The classic beta multiplier uses K:1 *current ratio* via the mirror (I2 = K·I1) but M1 and M2 have the SAME W/L. Then:
I1 = ½β(Vgs1-Vth)², I2 = ½β(Vgs2-Vth)² with I2 = K·I1 → (Vgs2-Vth) = √K·(Vgs1-Vth) → Vgs2 - Vgs1 = (√K - 1)(Vgs1-Vth) = (√K-1)·Vod1. ΔVgs = (√K-1)·Vod1. This is what appears across R3: I2·R3 = ΔVgs → I1·K·R3 = (√K-1)·Vod1 → self-consistent:
I1 = (√K-1)·Vod1/(K·R3) = (√K-1)·√(2I1/β)/(K·R3)
→ √I1 = (√K-1)·√(2/β)/(K·R3)
→ I1 = 2·(√K-1)²/(β·K²·R3²). 

The current is set by R3 (triode MOS). Its tempco: I ∝ 1/R² · 1/β. R3 ∝ 1/(μCox(Vgs-Vth))_triode... triode R = 1/(μCox(W/L)(Vgs-Vth)) at Vds small. So R3 ∝ 1/μ ∝ T^1.5 (since μ ∝ T^-1.5), and β ∝ μ ∝ T^-1.5. I1 ∝ (1/R3²)·(1/β) ∝ T^-3·T^1.5 = T^-1.5. So I1 ∝ T^-1.5 (CTAT-ish current). 

Now the output: VREF = Vgs of a diode-connected nfet fed by I1 (α = -1.5). dVod/dT = ((α+1.5)/2)·Vod/T = 0! So the output diode's *overdrive* has ZERO tempco (Vod ∝ T^0), because the CTAT current exactly cancels mobility tempco. VREF = Vth + Vod(const). dVREF/dT = dVth/dT ≈ -1.4 mV/°C. STILL negative — the CTAT diode dominates. Damn.

So beta-multiplier + diode output = CTAT (-1.4mV/°C) → ~1000 ppm/°C. Need PTAT compensation on top.

**The missing PTAT: use the ΔVgs itself!** VREF could be a node where a PTAT voltage (the ΔVgs across the triode resistor) is added in series with the CTAT diode. In the beta multiplier, the ΔVgs appears across R3. If we tap VREF = Vgs1 (CTAT) + ΔVgs (PTAT portion)... but ΔVgs in strong inversion is (√K-1)·Vod1 which is T^0.75 (weak PTAT, +0.75·ΔVgs/T). ΔVgs for K=4: (2-1)·Vod1 = Vod1 ≈ 0.3V → +0.75·0.3/300 = +0.75 mV/°C. Cancels about half of -1.4. VREF = Vgs1 + ΔVgs ≈ 0.8 + 0.3 = 1.1V. Tempco ≈ -0.65 mV/°C → ~550 ppm/°C. Not enough.

Need a stronger PTAT term. Options:
1. Increase K so ΔVgs bigger and T^0.75 coefficient grows? ΔVgs = (√K-1)·Vod1. dVgs_ptat/dT = 0.75·ΔVgs/T. To get +1.4 mV/°C: ΔVgs = 1.4e-3·300/0.75 = 0.56V. (√K-1)·Vod1 = 0.56 with Vod1 = 0.35 → √K-1 = 1.6 → K = 6.8. And VREF = Vgs1 + ΔVgs = (Vth+0.35) + 0.56 = 1.61V. Too high. Contradiction: bigger ΔVgs pushes VREF up.

2. **Operate the beta core in weak inversion** → ΔVgs = n·VT·ln(K·...) ∝ T (true PTAT). Then ΔVgs ≈ nVT ln(K²)... e.g. K=4 → ΔVgs = 1.5·26mV·ln(4·...)... weak-inversion ΔVgs = n·VT·ln(I2/I1 · W1/W2) = nVT·ln(K²) if mirror ratio K and device ratio inverse... For K=4: ln(16)=2.77, ΔVgs=1.5·26·2.77=108mV, d/dT = (1.5·0.086·2.77) mV/°C = 0.36 mV/°C. Still weak. For K=64: ln(4096)=8.3, ΔVgs=324mV, d/dT=1.07mV/°C. VREF ≈ Vgs1 + 0.324 ≈ 1.1V... tempco -1.4+1.07 = -0.33 mV/°C → 275ppm/°C. Close! With more tuning (increase n by using a well/body or bigger ratio) could reach ≤300ppm.

3. **Use the ΔVgs to generate a *current* that's injected into the output diode, with current gain.** This is the "PTAT current injection" method: generate a PTAT current (I_PTAT ∝ T) and mirror it with gain into the output diode so the output diode's overdrive grows with T, canceling dVth/dT. The current-driven compensation gives Vod ∝ T^{(α+1.5)/2} and with current gain the effective Vod can be large enough. But the output VREF = Vth + Vod stays ~1.05-1.15V.

Hmm. All paths end near VREF ≈ Vth + Vod + (small PTAT ΔVgs), max ~1.2V requires Vod ≈ 0.45-0.5V (Vth~0.7). To hit 1.2V with Vth~0.7, need Vod = 0.5V and it must be temperature-stable. Vod = √(2I/β). With I ∝ T^α and β ∝ T^-1.5: Vod ∝ T^{(α+1.5)/2}. For Vod to be *constant* vs T: α = -1.5. So a CTAT-ish current (-1.5 exponent) into the output diode gives constant Vod, VREF = Vth + const → tempco = dVth/dT ≈ -1.4mV/°C. Still the Vth problem. The ONLY way to cancel Vth's -1.4mV/°C is a PTAT term of +1.4mV/°C injected into VREF. With Vod=VREF-Vth, a PTAT current (α=1) gives dVod/dT = +1.25·Vod/T. Setting +1.25·Vod/300 = 1.4 → Vod = 336mV. VREF = Vth + 0.336 = 1.04-1.11V. To get VREF=1.2 need to raise Vth or add a second diode.

**Approach: two stacked CTAT diodes for the Vth, plus PTAT current through them.** VREF = Vgs1 + Vgs2 = 2·(Vth+Vod) ≈ 1.6-1.8V. Too high, and the PTAT compensation is also doubled... We want VREF = 1.2. 2·Vth = 1.4 already > 1.2. 

**Approach: Vgs_nfet + Vgs_pfet stacked.** PMOS Vth is different. Vgs_n(≈0.8) + Vgs_p(≈0.8) = 1.6. Too high.

**Approach: single diode + series PTAT ΔVgs with current-gain amplification.** The gain needed (as computed) is ~2-3× on the ΔVgs term. In MOS-only circuits, a ×2 gain on a voltage is hard without resistors.

**Approach: make Vth higher via longer L (short-channel effects reduce Vth; long L increases Vth slightly). Or use the body effect** — tie the diode nfet's bulk to a reverse-biased voltage to raise Vth. In GF180 the nfet_03v3 has source tied to VSS and bulk=VSS normally (4-terminal device: D G S B). The `.subckt` uses 4 nodes (D G S B). We can raise Vth of the output diode by connecting its bulk to a lower voltage... but VSS is already 0; can't go lower. Alternatively connect the output diode's bulk to a negative... no.

Alternatively, **use a diode-connected device whose Vgs is naturally ~1.2V at moderate current density.** Vgs = Vth + Vod. For gf180 nfet_03v3, Vth ≈ 0.7-0.8V. Vod for Vgs=1.2 → 0.4-0.5V. That's a decently large overdrive, achievable. So single diode CAN reach 1.2V. The tempco issue: we need the PTAT compensation to make dVgs/dT = -a + 1.25·Vod/T = 0 at Vod = 0.45: 1.25·0.45/300 = 1.875 mV/°C. Need dVth/dT = -1.875 mV/°C for exact cancel. Actual gf180 nfet dVth/dT might be around -1.3 to -1.5. So net +0.4 to +0.6 mV/°C → ~400-500 ppm/°C. Marginal. With a slightly weaker current exponent (α < 1) we can reduce: need (α+1.5)/2·Vod/T = a. For a=1.4, Vod=0.45: (α+1.5)/2 = 1.4e-3·300/0.45 = 0.933 → α+1.5 = 1.867 → α = 0.37. So an output current with α ≈ 0.4 (weakly PTAT) at Vod=0.45 gives near-zero tempco at 27°C and VREF≈1.15-1.25. 

**So: generate a current with tunable temperature exponent α ≈ 0.4 and feed it into a diode-connected nfet sized for Vod≈0.45V.** The α knob is the beta-multiplier K ratio + weak/strong inversion mix. 

Actually, I realize the cleanest way to get a controllable-α current is to **inject a PTAT current into the CTAT (constant or T^-1.5) current**: I_total = I_CTAT + I_PTAT, giving an α between -1.5 and +1 depending on the mix. But that needs the two current generators.

This is getting very involved. Let me step back and think about what's actually achievable with the tool budget, then just be **empirical**: I'll build a topology with a few tunable device sizes, run a sweep in ngspice, and numerically tune. Let me define the topology with clear knobs:

_Thinking:_

The batched harness works (~30s per candidate). v0 confirmed: tempco tiny (36ppm) but VREF=2.23V and line_reg terrible (624 mV/V). The VREF is way too high and supply-dependent.

Now let me design a proper topology. The key issues to fix:
1. VREF should be ~1.2V — a single diode-connected nfet gives ~0.8-1.0V, so I need to add a level (a small PTAT series term) OR make the diode have higher overdrive.
2. Line regulation ≤ 100 mV/V — need a proper self-biased current source with cascoded mirror so the current through the output diode is supply-independent.
3. Tempco ≤ 300 ppm/°C.

Let me now design the real topology — the **self-biased cascoded beta-multiplier core + temperature-compensated output**, but with MY distinct arrangement:

Key design (10-11 MOS):

```
.subckt vref_1v2 VDD VSS VREF IBIAS
* --- IBIAS input: diode-connected nfet (current-to-voltage) ---
XM0  ibg  ibg  VSS VSS nfet_03v3 L=2u W=4u nf=2

* --- Self-biased beta core, cascoded PMOS mirror ---
XM1  a    a    VDD  VDD pfet_03v3 L=2u W=4u nf=2   ; P diode (leg1 top)
XM2  b    a    VDD  VDD pfet_03v3 L=2u W=4u nf=2   ; P mirror (leg2 top)
XM3  na   a    b    b   pfet_03v3 L=2u W=4u nf=2   ; cascode leg1 ... 
```

Hmm wait. The standard cascoded beta-multiplier. Let me structure it cleanly:

Leg 1: node na. PMOS P1a (cascode, gate=pg), P1b (diode-connected, gate=drain=pg). 
Leg 2: node nb. PMOS P2a (cascode, gate=pg), P2b (diode? no, mirror, gate=pg).
P1b diode-connected sets pg. P1a cascodes P1b. P2b mirrors P1b.

For the NMOS beta pair: M1 diode at na, M2 at nb with triode R below.

To mirror a current to the high side, the classic: IBIAS into nfet diode XM0 sets V(ibg). A *second* nfet XM0b (gate=ibg) mirrors IBIAS from VSS: carries IBIAS·ratio sinking from its drain node. That drain node (call it pm) has a PMOS diode XM1 to VDD: current IBIAS·r flows through XM1 (diode), setting V(pm) = VDD - |Vgs,p|. Then XM2 (pmos, gate=pm) mirrors the current to the output. So:
```
XM0  ibg  ibg  VSS VSS nfet L=2u W=4u nf=2         ; IBIAS → V(ibg)
XM0b pm   ibg  VSS VSS nfet L=2u W=4u nf=2         ; mirror IBIAS from VSS (sinks from pm)

XM2  vref pm   VDD VDD pfet L=2u W=8u nf=2         ; output mirror
XM3  vref vref VSS VSS nfet L=2u W=1u nf=1         ; output diode
```
Total current through XM0b/XM1 = IBIAS·(ratio). Then XM2 mirrors (ratio2) into output diode. IDD = IBIAS + mirrored branch + output branch ≈ 20 + 20 + 40 = 80µA. Under 300µA. 

The supply current excludes IBIAS, so IDD (from VDD) = branch + output ≈ 60µA. Good.

To get ≤100mV/V (≤60mV over 3.0-3.6V), the current into the output diode must be very stable. dVREF/dI = 1/gm(XM3). With gm(XM3) ~ 60µS, dVREF/dI = 16.7mV/µA. For ≤60mV over 0.6V → ΔI ≤ 3.6µA → current stability ≤3.6µA over 0.6V supply → ~6% for 60µA. Achievable with cascoding.

Node IBIAS connects to: IIB source, XM0's drain/gate (ibg). So the 20µA must flow through XM0. If XM0 is off (Vgs≈0), then it's in subthreshold — V(ibg) would rise to conduct 20µA via subthreshold... V(ibg)=1.3e-16 ≈ 0, so XM0 must be conducting via... 

Oh! I bet the problem is ngspice converged to a degenerate solution because the operating point has TWO stable states and it landed on the wrong one, OR — more likely — **the .op solution has a floating node issue**. Actually `x1.ibg = 1.3e-16` — a floating node indicator. Node ibg connects to XM0 (D,G) and nothing else. IIB forces IBIAS (the port). Wait — IBIAS is the subckt port, connected to XM0 drain/gate inside and IIB outside. V(IBIAS)=V(x1.ibg) since they're the same net. But `v(x1.ibg)=1.3e-16` while IIB sources 20µA... that's impossible unless XM0 conducts 20µA at Vgs≈0 (impossible). Unless ngspice didn't converge and gave a garbage solution. The vdd#branch = -1.1e-11 means XM2 conducts ~11pA — everything is off. Total circuit current ~11pA. So IBIAS's 20µA... `vssv#branch = 1.1e-11`. Total from VSS is 11pA. So the 20µA from IIB goes NOWHERE?? That means KCL is violated — the op point didn't converge. ngspice should report non-convergence.

Wait — actually maybe there's a netlist issue: I wrote `XM0  ibg  ibg  VSS  VSS  nfet_03v3 L=2u W=4u nf=2`. In the subckt, port names are VDD VSS VREF IBIAS. Inside the subckt, node `VSS` refers to the port VSS. Fine. `XM0` gate=drain=ibg, source=VSS (port), bulk=VSS. OK.

Hmm, but node `ibg` — is it connected to the IBIAS port? The subckt is `.subckt vref_1v2 VDD VSS VREF IBIAS`. XM0's drain is `ibg`, which is an internal node, NOT the IBIAS port! IBIAS (the port) is connected to... nothing inside the subckt! My netlist has XM0's drain at internal node `ibg`, and the port `IBIAS` is floating (only connected to the IIB source outside). 

THAT'S the bug. The IBIAS port must connect to XM0's drain. I need the drain node to BE IBIAS (the port), not `ibg`. Let me fix: XM0 drain = IBIAS, gate = IBIAS.

So the correct netlist:
```
XM0  IBIAS IBIAS VSS VSS nfet_03v3 L=2u W=4u nf=2
XM0b pm   IBIAS VSS VSS nfet_03v3 L=2u W=4u nf=2
```
And v(IBIAS) is now the port. That's why vref was 0.26 — the port IBIAS was floating (11pA leakage), the output diode at ~11pA → vref = Vgs at 11pA ≈ subthreshold ~0.26V. And it was "flat" vs IBIAS because IBIAS was disconnected.

For it to conduct, current flows FROM source (3.3) THROUGH to drain (P) — i.e., the pfet must SOURCE current. But IIB sources current INTO P (from P to 0). That's backwards for the pfet. So for the nfet diode d1: IIB 0 N DC 20u pushes 20µA INTO N → flows through diode to 0. V(N)=0.94V. Correct. That's what my harness does (IIB IBIAS 0 DC 20u). Good.

_Thinking:_

Even with nodeset, it lands at the same degenerate solution: v(ibias) = -0.56V. And the ramp produced no output for the print (maybe the print after dc needs different syntax, or the dc sweep also failed).

v(ibias) = -0.56V is really suspicious. A diode with gate=drain at -0.56V and source at 0V shouldn't conduct 20µA. But KCL says IIB's 20µA must go through XM0. Unless the diode IS conducting via a different mechanism...

OH WAIT. I think I finally get it. **`XM0  IBIAS IBIAS VSS  VSS` — the drain and gate are BOTH at IBIAS. But which terminal is "drain" and which is "source" depends on the node voltages, and ngspice with a symmetric MOS model (default) can swap.** At V(IBIAS)=-0.56, the device's source (lower potential) is actually the IBIAS terminal, and drain = VSS = 0. So current flows from 0 (VSS) through the "swapped" channel to IBIAS=-0.56 and out through IIB... to 0. That's a complete loop of 20µA with the device conducting in REVERSE as if Vgs_effective = 0 - (-0.56)... = +0.56V?? No — for a swapped device, Vgs = V(G)-V(S) where S is the lower terminal = IBIAS. Vgs = -0.56 - (-0.56) = 0. Still off.

Hmm, actually let me reconsider — maybe the issue is simpler: **the current source IIB polarity**. `IIB IBIAS 0 DC 20u` — in SPICE, a current source `NAME N+ N- DC value` pushes current from N+ through the source to N-. So IIB pushes 20µA from IBIAS to 0. So it REMOVES 20µA from node IBIAS into ground. That means it's a current SOURCE pulling current OUT of IBIAS node down to ground. For the diode XM0 to supply that current (from VSS=0 up to IBIAS)... the diode is oriented from IBIAS (anode) to VSS (cathode), so current flows INTO IBIAS through the diode and out through IIB. So IIB should be oriented as a *load* (sinking current from IBIAS to ground). `IIB IBIAS 0 DC 20u` = 20µA from IBIAS to 0 = sinking. That's correct for an nfet diode: current flows into the diode's drain (IBIAS), through to source (VSS), and IIB sinks it. So V(IBIAS) should be +0.94 (like d1). 

But it's -0.56. The presence of XM0b (gate tied to IBIAS) changes things. With V(IBIAS)=-0.56V, XM0b (gate=-0.56) is off. And XM0 conducts reverse...

I wonder if this is an ngspice multi-solution issue where it found a stable-but-wrong solution because the model has some weird characteristic, OR the `.temp`/model lib has a bug with this topology.

Let me try a completely different, more robust measurement approach: **transient ramp**. Power up VDD over a few µs and also let IBIAS come up, then sample VREF at steady state. This reliably finds the physical operating point. Let me test with `.tran`:

IIB PWL from 0 to 20u
.tran 0.1u 20u
print v(vref) at last
```
Let me test. Actually simpler: use `.dc` with a small gmin or use `options` — but let me try tran ramp.

Actually, let me reconsider. In d1 (single diode), V(N)=0.94. Let me ADD just XM0b (mirror) + a PMOS diode XM1 (the pfet diode sourcing current) and see. The d3 test showed a pfet diode with IIB P 0 DC 20u DIVERGES (V=-1e7) — because IIB pushes current INTO P, but the pfet diode sources current OUT of P. So in my CASC circuit, the pmos diode XM1 sources current INTO pm, and XM0b sinks it — correct. But maybe ngspice's solver treats the combination badly.

The op solver sticks to a degenerate solution. Let me try a transient power-up ramp which physically finds the right state.

## Assistant (Build · DeepSeek V4 Flash · 13.6s)

_Thinking:_

The transient ramp also gives vref ≈ 0.013V. So the physical solution is that the circuit is essentially OFF. VREF=13mV means the output diode conducts nothing. V(ibias) and V(pm) didn't print (print after tran gives final values only if syntax right — didn't show).

So the circuit genuinely doesn't start. Why? Let me think about the startup. When VDD ramps to 3.3:
- XM0 (nfet diode at IBIAS) conducts 20µA (IIB). V(IBIAS) ≈ 0.94V.
- XM0b (gate=IBIAS=0.94) mirrors 20µA: sinks 20µA from pm.
- XM1 (pfet diode): sources current from VDD into pm. For it to source 20µA, V(pm) = VDD - |Vgs1(20µA)| ≈ 3.3-0.9 = 2.4V. 
- XM2 (pfet, gate=pm=2.4): Vgs = 2.4-3.3 = -0.9V → sources ~40µA (W=8µ, ratio 2× XM1) into vref.
- XM3 (diode at 40µA): VREF = Vgs3 ≈ 0.9-1.1V.

This SHOULD work. But simulation says VREF=13mV. So somewhere in the chain current is not flowing. Since V(pm) not printed, let me check. The most likely failure: **XM0b is NOT mirroring**. If V(IBIAS)=0.94V, XM0b conducts 20µA from pm. But wait — what if ngspice's model for `nfet_03v3` with `nf=2` on XM0b... both XM0 and XM0b have nf=2, W=4u. Same.

Hmm, unless the issue is that the PMOS diode XM1 (gate=drain=pm) with source=bulk=VDD — when V(pm) is close to VDD at startup (before current flows), XM1 is off, so pm floats high, XM0b's drain... XM0b needs to sink 20µA but there's no source of current into pm at startup → pm stays near VDD? No: XM0b sinks from pm → pm drops → XM1 turns on → equilibrium at 2.4V. 

Wait, unless V(IBIAS) is NOT 0.94 because IIB is fighting. Let me reconsider IIB direction once more. `IIB IBIAS 0 DC 20u`: current source from IBIAS to 0. SPICE convention: current flows from the first node through the source to the second node. So IIB draws current from IBIAS node toward ground = sinks 20µA from IBIAS. Diode XM0 must then SOURCE 20µA into IBIAS (from VSS through the diode). For an nfet diode (drain=gate=IBIAS, source=VSS), current flows INTO the drain from IBIAS... no wait: the nfet's drain is at IBIAS, source at VSS. Current flows from drain (higher) to source (lower) when on. So current flows IBIAS → (through channel) → VSS. That means XM0 PUSHES current OUT of IBIAS into VSS. But IIB also wants to PULL current out of IBIAS into ground. Both can't push/pull the same node — there's a conflict: IIB sinks 20µA from IBIAS, XM0 sinks 20µA from IBIAS → 40µA total must come from somewhere → from where? The gates of XM0/XM0b (no current). So IBIAS node has two current sinks and no source → V(IBIAS) collapses negative. THAT'S THE BUG!

The d1 test worked because... `XM0 N N 0 0 nfet L=1u W=4u` + `IIB 0 N DC 20u`: IIB sources current FROM 0 INTO N (direction: 0→N through source). So IIB PUSHES 20µA INTO node N. Diode XM0 (drain=gate=N, source=0) sinks it to ground. Correct! In d1 I wrote `IIB 0 N DC 20u` (from 0 to N). 

In my harness/CASC I wrote `IIB IBIAS 0 DC 20u` (from IBIAS to 0) — the REVERSE direction! That pushes current OUT of IBIAS into 0, fighting the diode. THAT'S the bug — my testbench polarity was wrong for the nfet-diode convention. 

So: **`IIB 0 IBIAS DC 20u`** sources current INTO the IBIAS node (from ground), which the nfet diode sinks. Let me fix the harness: change `IIB IBIAS 0 DC {ibias}` to `IIB 0 IBIAS DC {ibias}`.

The direction convention: "IBIAS is an external bias current input" — a current injected into the chip. With an nfet diode on IBIAS sinking to VSS, the current is injected INTO the IBIAS node. So the testbench should source into IBIAS. My harness was wrong. Let me fix explore.py build_deck.

This explains EVERYTHING: the flat vref vs IBIAS (because IBIAS never actually flowed into the circuit), the degenerate states.

Let me fix and re-run.

Found the real bug: my testbench current source polarity was reversed (`IIB IBIAS 0` pushes current *out* of the node, fighting the nfet diode). It must be `IIB 0 IBIAS` to inject into the chip. Let me fix the harness.

{
  "command": "cd \"$MBG_ROOT/results/deepseek_vref_1v2\" && source \"$MBG_ROOT/.venv/bin/activate\" && timeout 500 python -c \"\nfrom explore import measure\n# Self-biased beta core, seeded by IBIAS, output diode from mirror of core current\nfor trL,trW in [('2u','4u'),('2u','1u'),('4u','1u')]:\n  for OL,OW in [('0.28u','0.5u'),('0.28u','1u'),('0.5u','1u')]:\n    net = '''\n.subckt vref_1v2 VDD VSS VREF IBIAS\nXM0  IBIAS IBIAS VSS VSS nfet_03v3 L=2u W=4u nf=2\nXM0b na   IBIAS VSS VSS nfet_03v3 L=2u W=4u nf=2\nXM1  na   na   VDD VDD pfet_03v3 L=2u W=4u nf=2\nXM2  nb   na   VDD VDD pfet_03v3 L=2u W=12u nf=2\nXM3  na   na   VSS VSS nfet_03v3 L=2u W=4u nf=2\nXM4  nb   na   x   VSS nfet_03v3 L=2u W=4u nf=2\nXM5  x    VDD  VSS VSS nfet_03v3 L=%s W=%s nf=2\nXM6  vref na   VDD VDD pfet_03v3 L=2u W=4u nf=2\nXM7  vref vref VSS VSS nfet_03v3 L=%s W=%s nf=1\n.ends\n''' % (trL,trW,OL,OW)\n    r = measure(net, workdir='/tmp/opencode/vref_sim_selfb')\n    if r:\n        print('triode L=%s W=%s out L=%s W=%s | v27=%.4f tc=%6.0fppm line=%s | @10u=%s @30u=%s' % (\n            trL,trW,OL,OW,r['vref_27'], r['tempco_ppmC'],\n            '%.0f'%r['line_reg_mV/V'] if r['line_reg_mV/V'] else 'N/A',\n            '%.4f'%r['vref@10u'] if r['vref@10u'] else 'N/A',\n            '%.4f'%r['vref@30u'] if r['vref@30u'] else 'N/A'))\n\" 2>&1 | tail -12"
}
```

_Thinking:_

Progress! The self-biased core:
- tempco 112 ppm (excellent!) at L=0.28u W=0.5u, v27=1.355
- IBIAS sensitivity: @10u=1.319, @30u=1.394 → swing 75mV over 20µA = 3.75mV/µA. MUCH better than 24mV/µA! (from ±125mV to ±37mV).
- BUT line_reg = 412 mV/V — TERRIBLE! The core current is VDD-dependent.

Line reg problem: the beta core's current depends on VDD because... the triode MOS M5 (gate=VDD) resistance changes with VDD (Vgs=VDD-0). As VDD changes, R_triode changes → current changes. That's the classic weakness of using gate=VDD triode MOS. Fix: bias the triode gate with a VDD-independent voltage (e.g., from a diode), or cascade the output, or use a feedback-regulated mirror.

The prior repo's vref also had line_reg 304 mV/V — same problem (gate=VDD triode).

To fix line reg with a gate=VDD triode: the triode R ∝ 1/(μCox(W/L)(Vgs-Vth)) with Vgs=VDD. As VDD varies 3.0→3.6, Vgs varies 3.0→3.6 → R changes ~20% → current changes → VREF changes. Need Vgs of triode to be supply-independent.

**Fix: bias the triode gate from the reference's own node** — a "regulated" approach. Standard technique: use a self-biased triode whose gate is tied to a node that's supply-independent, e.g., the drain of the PMOS diode (na) or the output node itself (self-regulated).

If the triode gate = V(na) (the PMOS-diode node, which is ~VDD-|Vgs|), then as VDD rises, V(na) rises too (tracks VDD) → Vgs_triode = V(na) ≈ VDD-0.9 → still VDD-dependent. Hmm.

Better: tie the triode gate to a voltage derived from the ΔVgs reference itself that's constant. E.g., the output diode's gate (vref) — a VDD-independent voltage! VREF is ~1.2-1.35V, fixed. If triode gate = VREF: Vgs_triode = VREF - V(x)... VREF is constant → triode R constant → current supply-independent. This is a **self-regulated beta multiplier** (common technique: "gate connected to the regulated node").

Let me try triode gate = VREF. But wait — that creates a feedback: VREF drives triode which sets current which sets VREF. Should settle. Let me test.

Actually, simpler and very robust: **cascode the output mirror and use a longer-L core**, and measure. But the real issue is the triode's gate. Let me try gate=VREF first, and also try gate = V(na) + something. Let me also try replacing the triode with a diode-connected pfet... no.

Actually, another classic: the triode MOS with **gate tied to VSS through... ** no, gate must be high to be on.

Let me test: triode gate = VREF (self-regulated).

Also, I should reconsider the tempco target: current best tc=112ppm, need ≤300. Good margin. VREF=1.355 — need 1.2. The output diode needs more current or different size. Let me tune.

}

No. For PMOS: the "drain" of a pfet mirror device connects to the load. The cascode device is placed between the mirror device's drain and the load:
```

But what if the input diode carries a current MUCH larger than IBIAS — i.e., the IBIAS current is *amplified* before the "input diode" that sets the reference?

Redefine: IBIAS → nfet mirror XM0/XM0b with ratio r0 (XM0b W = r0·XM0 W). So the current into the pmos-diode branch = r0·IBIAS. Then the "IBIAS_nom" in the sens formula becomes r0·IBIAS:
sens = Vod3/(2·r0·IBIAS_nom). With r0=4: sens = 0.5/(2·80µ) = 3.1mV/µA → over 10-30µA: ±31mV. VREF@10µA≈1.19, @30µA≈1.25 — inside window!

{

}

  "command": "cd \"$MBG_ROOT/results/deepseek_vref_1v2\" && source \"$MBG_ROOT/.venv/bin/activate\" && timeout 900 python -c \"\nfrom explore import measure\nresults=[]\nfor OW in ['5u','8u','12u']:\n  for OL in ['0.28u','0.5u']:\n    net = '''\n.subckt vref_1v2 VDD VSS VREF IBIAS\nXM0  IBIAS IBIAS VSS  VSS  nfet_03v3 L=2u W=4u nf=2\nXM0b pm   IBIAS VSS  VSS  nfet_03v3 L=2u W=4u nf=2\nXM1  pm   pm   VDD  VDD  pfet_03v3 L=2u W=4u nf=2\nXM2  vref pm   VDD  VDD  pfet_03v3 L=2u W=8u nf=2\nXM3a vref vref m   VSS  nfet_03v3 L=%s W=%s nf=1\nXM3b m    m    VSS  VSS  nfet_03v3 L=%s W=%s nf=1\n.ends\n''' % (OL,OW,OL,OW)\n    r = measure(net, workdir='/tmp/opencode/vref_sim_stack')\n    if r:\n        results.append((r, OL, OW))\nfor r,OL,OW in sorted(results, key=lambda x: (abs(x[0]['vref_27']-1.2), x[0]['tempco_ppmC'])):\n    print('outL=%s W=%s | v27=%.4f tc=%6.0f line=%6.1f idd=%s | @10u=%s @30u=%s' % (\n        OL,OW,r['vref_27'], r['tempco_ppmC'], r['line_reg_mV/V'] or -1,\n        ('%.0fu'%(r['idd_A']*1e6)) if r['idd_A'] else 'N/A',\n        '%.4f'%r['vref@10u'] if r['vref@10u'] else 'N/A',\n        '%.4f'%r['vref@30u'] if r['vref@30u'] else 'N/A'))\n\" 2>&1 | tail -8"
}

{
  "command": "cd \"$MBG_ROOT/results/deepseek_vref_1v2\" && source \"$MBG_ROOT/.venv/bin/activate\" && timeout 900 python -c \"\nfrom explore import measure\nresults=[]\nfor OL,OW in [('0.5u','1.2u'),('0.5u','1.5u'),('0.5u','2u'),('1u','2.5u'),('1u','3u'),('2u','4u')]:\n    net = '''\n.subckt vref_1v2 VDD VSS VREF IBIAS\nXM0  IBIAS IBIAS VSS  VSS  nfet_03v3 L=2u W=4u nf=2\nXM0b pm   IBIAS VSS  VSS  nfet_03v3 L=2u W=4u nf=2\nXM1  pm   pm   VDD  VDD  pfet_03v3 L=2u W=4u nf=2\nXM2  vref pm   VDD  VDD  pfet_03v3 L=2u W=8u nf=2\nXM3  vref vref VSS  VSS  nfet_03v3 L=%s W=%s nf=1\n.ends\n''' % (OL,OW)\n    r = measure(net, workdir='/tmp/opencode/vref_sim_cascL')\n    if r:\n        results.append((r, OL, OW))\nfor r,OL,OW in sorted(results, key=lambda x: (abs(x[0]['vref_27']-1.2), x[0]['tempco_ppmC'])):\n    print('outL=%s W=%s | v27=%.4f tc=%6.0f line=%6.1f idd=%s | @10u=%s @30u=%s' % (\n        OL,OW,r['vref_27'], r['tempco_ppmC'], r['line_reg_mV/V'] or -1,\n        ('%.0fu'%(r['idd_A']*1e6)) if r['idd_A'] else 'N/A',\n        '%.4f'%r['vref@10u'] if r['vref@10u'] else 'N/A',\n        '%.4f'%r['vref@30u'] if r['vref@30u'] else 'N/A'))\n\" 2>&1 | tail -8"
}

  "command": "cd \"$MBG_ROOT/results/deepseek_vref_1v2\" && source \"$MBG_ROOT/.venv/bin/activate\" && timeout 600 python -c \"\nfrom explore import build_deck, _read_dat\nfrom mbg.simulation import run_spice\nimport os, re\n\ndef pvt(net, wd, corner='typical'):\n    # returns dict: tempco, vref@9 PVT points, idd\n    temps=[-40,0,27,60,90,125]\n    out={}\n    # tempco at 3.3V\n    rows=None\n    for t in temps:\n        os.makedirs(wd, exist_ok=True)\n        for f in ('temp.dat','vdd.dat','ib.dat'):\n            p=os.path.join(wd,f)\n            if os.path.isfile(p): os.remove(p)\n        deck=build_deck(net, temp=t, corner=corner)\n        r=run_spice(deck, workdir=wd, timeout=200, fmt='dat')\n        so=r.get('stdout','')\n        d={}\n        for m in re.finditer(r'([\\w()#.]+)\\s*=\\s*(-?\\d*\\.?\\d*[eE][+-]?\\d*)',so):\n            d[m.group(1)]=float(m.group(2))\n        vref=None\n        tr=_read_dat(os.path.join(wd,'temp.dat'))\n        if tr: vref=tr[0][1]\n        if vref is None and 'v(vref)' in d: vref=d['v(vref)']\n        if vref is None: \n            print('  !! no vref at T=%g corner=%s'%(t,corner)); return None\n        out[t]=vref\n        if t==27:\n            out['idd']=d.get('vdd#branch')\n    tc=(max(out[t] for t in temps)-min(out[t] for t in temps))/(1.2*165.0)*1e6\n    out['tc']=tc\n    return out\n\nnet = '''\n.subckt vref_1v2 VDD VSS VREF IBIAS\nXM0  IBIAS IBIAS VSS  VSS  nfet_03v3 L=2u W=4u nf=2\nXM0b pm   IBIAS VSS  VSS  nfet_03v3 L=2u W=4u nf=2\nXM1  pm   pm   VDD  VDD  pfet_03v3 L=2u W=4u nf=2\nXM2  vref pm   VDD  VDD  pfet_03v3 L=2u W=8u nf=2\nXM3  vref vref VSS  VSS  nfet_03v3 L=0.5u W=1.2u nf=1\n.ends\n'''\nfor corner in ['typical','ff','ss']:\n    r=pvt(net, '/tmp/opencode/vref_pvt2_'+corner, corner)\n    if r:\n        v=r\n        print('%-9s tc=%6.0fppm | vref: %s | idd27=%s' % (corner, v['tc'],\n            ' '.join('T%g=%.3f'%(t,v[t]) for t in [-40,27,125]),\n            ('%.0fuA'%(abs(v['idd'])*1e6)) if v.get('idd') else 'N/A'))\n\" 2>&1 | tail -6"

  "command": "cd \"$MBG_ROOT/results/deepseek_vref_1v2\" && source \"$MBG_ROOT/.venv/bin/activate\" && timeout 600 python -c \"\nfrom mbg.simulation import run_spice\nimport os, re\n\nLIB='/home/huda/.volare/gf180mcuD/libs.tech/ngspice/sm141064.ngspice'\nDES='/home/huda/.volare/gf180mcuD/libs.tech/ngspice/design.ngspice'\n\ndef op_point(net, corner, temp, vdd=3.3, ibias=20e-6):\n    body=[]\n    body.append('.include \\\"%s\\\"'%DES)\n    body.append('.lib \\\"%s\\\" %s'%(LIB,corner))\n    body.append('.temp %g'%temp)\n    body.append(net.strip())\n    body.append('VDD VDD 0 %g'%vdd)\n    body.append('VSSV VSS 0 0')\n    body.append('IIB 0 IBIAS DC %g'%ibias)\n    body.append('CL VREF 0 1p')\n    body.append('X1 VDD VSS VREF IBIAS vref_1v2')\n    body.append('.control')\n    body.append('op')\n    body.append('print v(vref)')\n    body.append('.endc')\n    body.append('.end')\n    r=run_spice('\\n'.join(body), workdir='/tmp/opencode/vref_fast', timeout=120)\n    so=r.get('stdout','')\n    m=re.search(r'v\\(vref\\)\\s*=\\s*(-?\\d*\\.?\\d*[eE][+-]?\\d*)',so)\n    return float(m.group(1)) if m else None\n\nnet = '''\n.subckt vref_1v2 VDD VSS VREF IBIAS\nXM0  IBIAS IBIAS VSS  VSS  nfet_03v3 L=2u W=4u nf=2\nXM0b pm   IBIAS VSS  VSS  nfet_03v3 L=2u W=4u nf=2\nXM1  pm   pm   VDD  VDD  pfet_03v3 L=2u W=4u nf=2\nXM2  vref pm   VDD  VDD  pfet_03v3 L=2u W=8u nf=2\nXM3  vref vref VSS  VSS  nfet_03v3 L=0.5u W=1.2u nf=1\n.ends\n'''\ntemps=[-40,0,27,60,90,125]\nfor corner in ['typical','ff','ss']:\n    vs={}\n    for t in temps:\n        v=op_point(net,corner,t)\n        vs[t]=v\n    tc=(max(vs.values())-min(vs.values()))/(1.2*165.0)*1e6\n    print('%-9s tc=%6.0fppm | %s' % (corner,tc,' '.join('T%d=%.3f'%(t,vs[t]) for t in temps)))\n# VDD sweep at corners (line reg) - quick op at 3.0/3.6\nprint('--- line reg @27 ---')\nfor corner in ['typical','ff','ss']:\n    vlo=op_point(net,corner,27,vdd=3.0); vhi=op_point(net,corner,27,vdd=3.6)\n    print('%-9s vdd3.0=%.4f vdd3.6=%.4f line=%5.1fmV/V'%(corner,vlo,vhi,(vhi-vlo)/0.6*1000))\n\" 2>&1 | tail -10"
}

The line-reg issue: V(na) tracks VDD. The core current rose 34% over 0.6V VDD. The reason V(na) tracks VDD: V(na) = VDD - |Vgs1|. If |Vgs1| were CONSTANT, V(na) tracking VDD is expected AND FINE — what matters is whether the current is constant. The current I(XM3) at V(na) rising 0.19V: Vod3 rises 0.19V → current rises ~sqrt → 34% rise. So the problem is the core current is set by V(na) (the diode), and V(na) rises with VDD.

Why did it rise? ΔVgs = Vgs3 - Vgs4 = V(x). As current rises, Vod3 rises → ΔVgs = (√K-1)Vod3 rises. Circular. The instability came from the triode gate: Vgs5 = VREF - V(x), and VREF rose → R5 dropped → current rose. So the loop was: VDD↑ → VREF↑ → R5↓ → I↑ → VREF↑. Positive feedback through the triode gate=VREF.

If instead the triode gate is a truly FIXED voltage (independent of both VDD and VREF), R5 is constant → I is fixed by ΔVgs/R5 → line reg good. The fixed voltage could be V(IBIAS) (=1.06V, set by IBIAS current — supply independent!). Let me test: triode gate = V(IBIAS).

**FINAL DESIGN (results/deepseek_vref_1v2):**

```
.subckt vref_1v2 VDD VSS VREF IBIAS
* IBIAS input stage: diode-connected nfet converts external current to a
* supply-independent gate voltage, mirrored into the PMOS reference leg.
XM0  IBIAS IBIAS VSS  VSS  nfet_03v3 L=2u W=4u nf=2
XM0b pm   IBIAS VSS  VSS  nfet_03v3 L=2u W=4u nf=2
* PMOS diode (XM1) sets the mirror gate; XM2 mirrors into the output diode.
XM1  pm   pm   VDD  VDD  pfet_03v3 L=2u W=4u nf=2
XM2  vref pm   VDD  VDD  pfet_03v3 L=2u W=8u nf=2
* Output: diode-connected NMOS establishes the MOS-only 1.2V reference.
XM3  vref vref VSS  VSS  nfet_03v3 L=0.5u W=1.2u nf=1
.ends
```

319: class LineSearchStrategy(SearchStrategy):
320:     """Continue along a direction that already produced an improvement.
321: 
322:     This is the move the old optimizer was missing. Once a step helps, the
323:     next iteration extends it *and* brackets it, so the search can overshoot
324:     and come back rather than stopping at the first improvement.

326:     name = "line_search"
327: 
328:     def propose(self, state: SearchState, budget: int) -> List[Candidate]:
329:         accepted = [t for t in state.memory.trials
330:                     if t["decision"] == "ACCEPT" and t["params"].get("knob") == "width"]
331:         if not accepted:
332:             return []
333:         last = accepted[-1]
334:         f = float(last["params"].get("factor", 1.0))
335:         if f == 1.0:
336:             return []
337:         # Extend, repeat, and half-step: bracket the optimum instead of
338:         # assuming the trend continues. The measured sweep is non-monotonic,
339:         # so extrapolation alone would be wrong.
340:         step_sizes = [f * f, f, 1.0 - (1.0 - f) / 2.0] if f < 1 else [f * f, f,
341:                                                                      1.0 + (f - 1.0) / 2.0]
342:         out: List[Candidate] = []
343:         for i, nf in enumerate(step_sizes[:budget]):
344:             nf = round(nf, 4)
345:             if abs(nf - 1.0) < 1e-6:
346:                 continue
347:             out.append(_apply_width(
348:                 state.design, nf, f"L{state.iteration}.{i + 1}",
349:                 hypothesis=("the previous step improved the objective, so the "
350:                             "optimum lies further along this direction"),
351:                 source=self.name,
352:                 expected="continue the measured improvement",
353:                 risk="overshoot may break a currently passing spec"))
354:         return out
355: 
356: 
357: class SensitivityStrategy(SearchStrategy):
358:     """Size the next step from measured sensitivity instead of a fixed constant.
359: 
360:     Uses the recorded d(metric)/d(knob) to estimate the step that would close
361:     the largest remaining violation, then proposes that step plus a
362:     conservative half of it. Only fires once a sensitivity has been measured.
363:     """
364:     name = "sensitivity"
365: 
366:     def propose(self, state: SearchState, budget: int) -> List[Candidate]:
367:         if not state.report:
368:             return []
369:         worst = None
370:         for r in state.report.results:
371:             if r.required and r.status == "FAIL" and r.value and r.target:
372:                 need = 100.0 * (r.target - r.value) / abs(r.value)
373:                 if worst is None or abs(need) > abs(worst[1]):
374:                     worst = (r.name, need)
375:         if not worst:
376:             return []
377:         metric, need_pct = worst
378:         s = state.memory.sensitivity("width", metric)
379:         if not s:
380:             return []
381:         step_pct = need_pct / s                     # % change in width needed
382:         step_pct = max(-40.0, min(40.0, step_pct))  # stay in a sane range
383:         factor = round(1.0 + step_pct / 100.0, 4)
384:         if abs(factor - 1.0) < 1e-3:
385:             return []
386:         out = [_apply_width(
387:             state.design, factor, f"S{state.iteration}.1",
388:             hypothesis=(f"measured sensitivity d({metric})/d(width) = {s:.3g} "
389:                         f"%/% implies a {step_pct:+.1f}% width change closes "
390:                         f"the remaining {need_pct:+.1f}% gap"),
391:             source=self.name,
392:             expected=f"close the {metric} violation in one step",
393:             risk="the local linear model may not hold that far out")]
394:         if budget > 1:
395:             half = round(1.0 + step_pct / 200.0, 4)
396:             if abs(half - 1.0) > 1e-3:
397:                 out.append(_apply_width(
398:                     state.design, half, f"S{state.iteration}.2",
399:                     hypothesis="half of the sensitivity-implied step, in case "
400:                                "the linear model overshoots",
401:                     source=self.name, expected=f"partially close {metric}",
402:                     risk="may not be enough"))
403:         return out
404: 
405: 
406: class CompositeStrategy(SearchStrategy):
407:     """Escalation: try cheap local moves first, widen the search on stagnation.
408: 
409:     Tier 1 continues a working direction or uses measured sensitivity.
410:     Tier 2 falls back to the heuristic directions.
411:     Tier 3 widens to a coarse bracket when local moves have stopped helping.
412:     """
413:     name = "composite"
414: 
415:     def __init__(self, strategies: Optional[Sequence[SearchStrategy]] = None):
416:         self.strategies = list(strategies or [
417:             SensitivityStrategy(), LineSearchStrategy(), HeuristicStrategy()])
418: 
419:     def propose(self, state: SearchState, budget: int) -> List[Candidate]:
420:         out: List[Candidate] = []
421:         seen: set = set()
422:         for strat in self.strategies:
423:             if len(out) >= budget:
424:                 break
425:             for c in strat.propose(state, budget - len(out)):
426:                 key = (c.params.get("knob"), c.params.get("factor"))
427:                 if key in seen or state.memory.exhausted(c.change):
428:                     continue
429:                 seen.add(key)
430:                 out.append(c)
431:                 if len(out) >= budget:
432:                     break
433: 
434:         if not out and state.tier >= 3:
435:             # Local search is stuck: bracket widely and let measurement pick.
436:             for i, f in enumerate((0.6, 0.75, 1.5)):
437:                 key = ("width", f)
438:                 if key in seen:
439:                     continue
440:                 out.append(_apply_width(
441:                     state.design, f, f"W{state.iteration}.{i + 1}",
442:                     hypothesis="local steps stagnated; sample a wider bracket "
443:                                "to escape the local region",
444:                     source="wide_bracket",
445:                     expected="find a better region of the design space",
446:                     risk="large moves may break several specs at once"))
447:         return out[:budget]
448: 
449: 
450: def default_strategy() -> SearchStrategy:
451:     return CompositeStrategy()
452: 
453: 
454: # ── Pareto archive ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
455: 
456: @dataclass
457: class ParetoArchive:
458:     """A small set of non-dominated designs, for later trade-off decisions."""
459:     entries: List[Dict[str, object]] = field(default_factory=list)
460:     limit: int = 8
461:

634: def _classify(flow: FlowResult, gate: SignoffResult,
635:               decision: Optional[Decision]) -> tuple:
636:     if flow is None:
637:         return AutoStatus.CONFIGURATION_FAILURE, "the flow never ran"
638:     if flow.failure == Failure.TOOL_FAILURE:
639:         return AutoStatus.TOOL_FAILURE, flow.message
640:     if flow.failure == Failure.CONFIGURATION_FAILURE:
641:         return AutoStatus.CONFIGURATION_FAILURE, flow.message
642:     if decision and decision.verdict == Verdict.ESCALATE:
643:         # A reviewer did not complete. Silence is not approval.
644:         return AutoStatus.BLOCKED, decision.reason
645:     if flow.failure == Failure.VERIFICATION_FAILURE:
646:         return AutoStatus.VERIFICATION_FAILURE, flow.message
647:     # BLOCKED is reserved for a run the evidence would otherwise have passed:
648:     # when the design simply did not converge, NOT_CONVERGED is both true and
649:     # more actionable, and the critical findings are listed in the report
650:     # either way. Saying "blocked" here would hide the real reason.
651:     if (decision and decision.verdict == Verdict.BLOCK and decision.blocking
652:             and flow.status not in (Outcome.NOT_CONVERGED, Outcome.FAIL)):
653:         return (AutoStatus.BLOCKED,
654:                 f"sign-off blocked by {len(decision.blocking)} unresolved "
655:                 f"CRITICAL finding(s): {decision.blocking[0].finding}")
656:     if gate.passed and flow.status == Outcome.PASS:
657:         return AutoStatus.SUCCESS, "all sign-off conditions met"
658:     if flow.status in (Outcome.NOT_CONVERGED, Outcome.FAIL):
659:         return AutoStatus.NOT_CONVERGED, flow.message or "specifications not met"
660:     return (AutoStatus.NOT_CONVERGED,
661:             "sign-off gate not satisfied: "
662:             + ", ".join(c["condition"] for c in (gate.failures + gate.not_run)))

778: 
779:     if summ["recommendations"]:
780:         L.append("\n### Recommendation trace\n")
781:         L.append("| recommendation | target | status | score before | after |")
782:         L.append("| --- | --- | --- | ---: | ---: |")
783:         for e in summ["recommendations"]:
784:             before = e["score_before"]
785:             after = e["score_after"]
786:             L.append(f"| {e['action']} | {e['target']} | {e['status']} | "
787:                      f"{before if before is None else round(before, 4)} | "
788:                      f"{after if after is None else round(after, 4)} |")
789:         L.append("")
790:         L.append("_Recommendations applied in the same iteration share one "
791:                  "before/after measurement, so the score change is attributed "
792:                  "to the batch, not to any single item. Isolating an "
793:                  "individual contribution needs one change per iteration._")
794: 
795:     L.append("\n## 9. Sign-Off Gate\n")
796:     if gate:
797:         L.append("```text\n" + gate.table() + "\n```")
798:         if gate.not_run:
799:             L.append("\nConditions reported `NOT RUN` were not evaluated. No "
800:                      "claim is made about them.")
801: 
802:     if not ok:
803:         L.append("\n## 10. What to do next\n")
804:         if flow and flow.best_pex:
805:             for f in flow.best_pex.failures:
806:                 L.append(f"- `{f.name}` still misses: {f.value:.4g} vs "
807:                          f"{f.op} {f.target:.4g}{f.unit}")
808:         acted = [e for e in summ["recommendations"]
809:                  if e["status"] in ("IMPROVED", "DEGRADED")]
810:         if acted:
811:             L.append("\nWhat helped and what did not:\n")
812:             for e in acted:
813:                 L.append(f"- {e['action']}: **{e['status']}**")
814:         L.append("\nRecommended manual intervention: review the unresolved "
815:                  "findings above, then either relax an infeasible target or "
816:                  "supply a stronger `tune_post` for the failing metric.")
817: 
818:     if res.package:
819:         L.append("\n## 11. Tapeout Files\n")
820:         for k, v in sorted(res.package.items()):
821:             L.append(f"- `{k}`: `{v}`")
822: 
823:     L.append("\n## 12. Reproduction\n")
824:     L.append("```text")
825:     L.append(f'{CANONICAL_COMMAND} "{(req.raw.strip() if req else "")[:200]}"')
826:     L.append("```")
827: 
828:     with open(path, "w") as f:
829:         f.write("\n".join(L) + "\n")
830:     return path
831: 
832: 
833: def _finalise(res: FullAutoResult, cfg: FullAutoConfig,
834:               ledger: ReviewLedger) -> None:
835:     try:
836:         ledger.write(os.path.join(cfg.outdir, "review_history.json"))
837:         with open(os.path.join(cfg.outdir, "full_auto_result.json"), "w") as f:

840:     except OSError:
841:         pass
842:     if cfg.verbosity >= 1:

844:         if res.signoff:

846:         print(f"\nSTATUS: {res.status}"

_Thinking:_

The extracted netlist has `.subckt vref_1v2 vref vss vdd` (ports reordered!) in the non-flat version, but the flat version has `.subckt vref_1v2 vdd vss vref`. My measure_all parses ports by name (vdd/vss/vref/ibias), so reordering is fine. But note: the extracted netlists use internal node names with `#` and no IBIAS port here (that was the 3-port design). My 4-port design should extract IBIAS properly (the pipeline labels pins).

Important: the extracted netlist uses `nfet_03v3`/`pfet_03v3` (aliases) — good, my .lib works. And it has `ad= pd= as= ps= w= l=` params on the X lines. My netlist parser for the layout (spice_to_gds) will also need to handle... no, that's the pipeline's job.

For measure_all, my deck builder wraps whatever netlist with the stimulus. The `.subckt` ports order doesn't matter since I instantiate `X1 VDD VSS VREF IBIAS vref_1v2` with named ports by position. Wait — I instantiate by POSITION (X1 VDD VSS VREF IBIAS vref_1v2), so the port order in the .subckt MUST match VDD VSS VREF IBIAS. The extracted netlist might reorder ports! Tuner logic (custom tune_post):
- get failing spec names from report.
- if `vref` failing (below 1.15): decrease XM3 W ×0.85 (higher Vod → higher vref). Also could increase mirror ratio.
- if `vref` failing (above 1.25): increase XM3 W ×1.2.
- if `tempco_ppmC` failing: change XM3 L (compensation knob): if tempco positive (VREF rises with T), increase L; if negative, decrease L. Or scale W.
- if `line_reg_mV_V` or `idd_ua` failing: scale mirror XM1/XM2 W ×0.8 (lower currents).

0.6)\n        netlist = _scale_width(netlist, \"XM3\", 1.1)\n        note.append(\"XM3 L=0.6u, W x1.1 (tempco)\")\n    if \"idd_ua\" in failing or \"line_reg_mV_V\" in failing:\n        netlist = _scale_width(netlist, \"XM1\", 0.8)\n        netlist = _scale_width(netlist, \"XM2\", 0.8)\n        note.append(\"mirror W x0.8 (reduce current)\")\n\n    step = int(design.circuit.get(\"_pex_step\", 0)) + 1\n    return design.evolve(\n        netlist=netlist,\n        layout=dict(design.layout or {}),\n        circuit={**design.circuit, \"_pex_step\": step},\n        note=f\"pex_tune_{step}({'; '.join(note) if note else 'no-op'})\")\n\n\ndef propose_candidates(*, design, report, degradation, iteration,\n                       baseline_score, budget):\n    \"\"\"Several distinct reference edits from the same baseline, each measured\n    independently so an improvement is attributable to one change.\"\"\"\n    from mbg.search import CandidateResult\n    failing = _failing(report)\n    candidates = []\n    v = report.get(\"vref\")\n    vval = v.value if v is not None else None\n\n    # Distinct hypotheses (each only fires if its target metric is relevant).\n    if \"tempco_ppmC\" in failing or (vval is not None and abs(vval - 1.2) > 0.03):\n        candidates.append((\"XM3 W x1.25\", _scale_width(design.netlist, \"XM3\", 1.25)))\n        candidates.append((\"XM3 W x0.8\", _scale_width(design.netlist, \"XM3\", 0.8)))\n        candidates.append((\"XM3 L=0.65u\", _set_l(design.netlist, \"XM3\", 0.65)))\n    elif \"idd_ua\" in failing or \"line_reg_mV_V\" in failing:\n        candidates.append((\"mirror W x0.8\", _scale_width(design.netlist, \"XM1\", 0.8)))\n        candidates.append((\"mirror W x0.85\", _scale_width(design.netlist, \"XM2\", 0.85)))\n        candidates.append((\"XM3 W x1.1\", _scale_width(design.netlist, \"XM3\", 1.1)))\n    else:\n        # Nothing obvious failing: bracket XM3 width to polish margin.\n        candidates.append((\"XM3 W x1.1\", _scale_width(design.netlist, \"XM3\", 1.1)))\n        candidates.append((\"XM3 W x0.9\", _scale_width(design.netlist, \"XM3\", 0.9)))\n        candidates.append((\"XM3 L=0.6u\", _set_l(design.netlist, \"XM3\", 0.6)))\n\n    candidates = candidates[:budget]\n    records = []\n    for i, (label, net) in enumerate(candidates):\n        cid = f\"C{iteration}.{i + 1}\"\n        cd = design.evolve(\n            netlist=net,\n            circuit={**design.circuit, \"_tag\": f\"cand_{cid}\"},\n            note=f\"{cid}:{label}\")\n        res = CandidateResult(candidate=cd)\n        try:\n            layout = hooks_ref[\"build_layout\"](cd)\n            if not layout.ok:\n                res.error = layout.message or \"layout failed\"\n                res.decision = \"ERROR\"\n                print(f\"[SEARCH]   {cid}: {res.error}\")\n                records.append(res.as_dict())\n                continue\n            metrics = hooks_ref[\"simulate_pex\"](cd, layout)\n            from mbg.specs import evaluate_specs\n            rep = evaluate_specs(metrics, ALL_SPECS, \"pex\")\n            res.ok, res.metrics, res.report = True, dict(metrics), rep\n            res.score, res.margin = rep.score, None\n            res.candidate.params[\"_layout\"] = layout\n            print(f\"[SEARCH]   {cid} ({label}): score {res.score:.4g}\"\n                  + (\"  PASSES\" if rep.passed else \"\")\n                  + f\" vref={metrics.get('vref')} tc={metrics.get('tempco_ppmC')}\")\n        except Exception as e:                       # noqa: BLE001\n            res.error = f\"{type(e).__name__}: {e}\"\n            res.decision = \"ERROR\"\n            print(f\"[SEARCH]   {cid}: ERROR {res.error}\")\n        records.append(res.as_dict())\n        if res.ok and res.score < baseline_score - 1e-9:\n            return cd, records\n    return None, records\n\n\n# ── PVT characterization (evidence, not a gate condition) ─────────────\ndef pvt_characterize(netlist, workdir):\n    \"\"\"Corners + temperature + supply behaviour. Writes pvt_characterization.json.\"\"\"\n    roles, ports = parse_ports(netlist, CELL)\n    results = {\"corners\": {}, \"tempco\": {}, \"ibias\": {}, \"supply\": {}}\n    temps = [-40, 0, 27, 60, 90, 125]\n    for corner in [\"typical\", \"ff\", \"ss\"]:\n        vs = {}\n        for t in temps:\n            deck = build_deck(netlist, CELL, temp=t, corner=corner)\n            wd = os.path.join(workdir, \"pvt\", corner)\n            os.makedirs(wd, exist_ok=True)\n            r = run_spice(deck, workdir=wd, timeout=400, fmt=\"dat\")\n            tr = _read_dat(os.path.join(wd, \"temp.dat\"))\n            v = tr[0][1] if tr else None\n            if v is None:\n                print(f\"[PVT] no vref at corner={corner} T={t}\")\n                continue\n            vs[t] = v\n        if not vs:\n            continue\n        tc = (max(vs.values()) - min(vs.values())) / (1.2 * 165.0) * 1e6\n        results[\"tempco\"][corner] = round(tc, 1)\n        results[\"corners\"][corner] = {str(t): round(v, 4) for t, v in vs.items()}\n    with open(os.path.join(workdir, \"pvt_characterization.json\"), \"w\") as f:\n        json.dump(results, f, indent=2)\n        f.write(\"\\n\")\n    return results\n\n\n# ── hooks ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\nhooks = make_hooks(\n    cell=CELL, in_node=\"VREF\", out_node=\"VREF\",\n    supplies={\"VDD\": 3.3, \"VSS\": 0.0},\n    spec_names=[s.name for s in ALL_SPECS],\n    specs=ALL_SPECS,\n    outdir=OUTDIR,\n    verbosity=1,\n)\nhooks.simulate_pre =     ok: bool = False
    metrics: Dict[str, float] = field(default_factory=dict)
    report: Optional[SpecReport] = None
    score: float = float("inf")

    def as_dict(self) -> Dict[str, object]:

                "margin_pct": round(self.margin, 3),
